On realizing that his elephantine form had been taken advantage of, Sambasadhana reverted to his original form and immediately yanked Kesari off his head with his hands. Although the fight continued for hours and hours, neither was able to outdo the other completely.

The damsel, however, wanted her ordeal to end. She knew that if Lord Shiva could help her by sending Kesari in the nick of time to stave off her attacker, then he could help her some more. She sat down yet again with her eyes closed and began to focus. Her ability to concentrate amid such chaos impressed the sages.50

“Blood!” She heard.

Blood? Did she hear it right?

She had neither any time nor opportunity to verify what she had heard. She knew that divine direction wasn’t usually repeated and that she had to decipher whatever it meant. She got up and started running toward where Kesari had initially appeared. There she found what she was looking for: his quiver of arrows and his bow. She quickly picked up the bow and an arrow and rushed to the combat area.

She ran fearlessly toward the fighting hulks. The villagers and sages gasped as they saw her racing in the direction of the fighters with a bow and an arrow. She stopped at a particular spot and crouched onto her knees. She carefully dipped the tip of the arrow in the puddle of blood that had dripped from the demon’s body. Despite being at each other’s throats, both Kesari and Sambasadhana noticed the girl’s action. But Kesari also caught the fleeting moment of panic in Sambasadhana’s eyes.51

Pulling himself away from the demon, Kesari ran toward the girl who stood arms outstretched with the bow and arrow. In one swift move, he grabbed the bow from her hand, nocked the arrow, and in an instant released it straight into Sambasadhana chest. As soon as the arrow hit the demon, he fell dead!

The sages and the villagers jumped up in joy hailing their savior Kesari. They lifted him on their shoulders and began to dance around. The girl’s eyes were brimming with tears – tears of joy and gratitude. After the mini celebration, the sages sat Kesari down in front of her. Kesari was just as grateful to her as she was to him. He pointed toward the girl and said, “The credit of killing Sambasadhana goes exclusively to this beautiful damsel whose name I do not know.”52

“Anjana!” they declared in chorus. Anjana stood blushing before him.

The sages proposed that it was apt that Kesari accept Anjana as his wife. The two stole glances at each other and smiled in agreement. The attraction was palpable.

“Through this marriage will be born a historic figure that will create a revolution in the world of devotion!” The sages thus blessed the happy couple.53

What a day – life, death, love, blessings, all bundled together.

retribution

As Anjana sat meditating inside the cave, she knew she had a long way to go before becoming pure enough to bear the divine destiny of the world in her womb. Echoing incessantly in her ears was the curse of her guru-father. (Or was he her father-guru? The lines had blurred considerably.) It was a sad day for her – a day she realized that she could never bear children.

It had been years since her marriage to the monkey-warrior Kesari. No matter how much they tried, she was just unable to conceive. Hope was waning from their lives. But every astrologer they consulted and every sage they sought blessings from assured them that soon, a divine being would be born through her womb. It took them some time to understand that the divine being wouldn’t be born the regular way and that they had to purify themselves in preparation for receiving the divine entity. While Kesari left on a pilgrimage of all the holy places, Anjana confined herself to a cave to perform severe austerities to please Lord Shiva.54

But she found it difficult to focus. Her mind kept wandering to the distant past. Every memory seemed so fresh, almost as if it had all happened yesterday, when in fact it had occurred in her previous life. If only she had reined in her temptations that day…

She was an apsara in the heavens, the favorite disciple of the celestial guru Brihaspati, and exquisitely beautiful. These were reasons enough to be called Managarva – one who is proud in her mind – by everyone. No one really called her by her real name Punjikasthali ever since Brihaspati, her guru, brought her to his ashram and raised her as his own, with discipline and love. He had handpicked her as soon as she was born, foreseeing in her a divine connect.55

When she turned sixteen, her mind began to rebel against not only the physical borders of the ashram she had been living in, but also the mental boundaries of her disciplined life, which had confined her. One day, she quietly sneaked out of the confines of the ashram with the hope of experiencing some thrill in the world outside. She soon reached a riverside. What she saw there remained etched in her mind like a leech sucking away her peace.56

A group of young men and women, surely gandharvas and apsaras, were engaged in amorous pastimes in the river. Seeing the couples, Punjikasthali’s mind began to reel. She was raised in a pure and protected environment, unaware of erotic feelings. Suddenly, she was exposed to sights beyond her wildest imagination. Her legs began to shake. Sweat streamed down her brows. Her lips began to quiver.

Tearing her eyes away from the scene, she rushed back into the protective fold of the ashram. No sooner had she entered, than her master’s glaring eyes met her physically and mentally disheveled form. The yogi that he was, he immediately read her mind.

“Your mind is flickering like a monkey desperately looking for opportunities of enjoyment. I curse you to become a monkey this instant!” Brihaspati was livid. He was so angry that his entire frame was quaking violently.

A crying and repentant Punjikasthali fell at her guru’s feet, begging forgiveness. What hurt her more than the curse was the look of disappointment on his face. She knew he had great dreams for her, and she had let him down through this careless lack of mental control and this momentary fall into a mental abyss. His plan for her required her to be pure at all levels.57

Although Brihaspati assured her that his curse was actually a blessing in disguise, Punjikasthali knew that she would have to purify herself intensely to be able to receive the hope of the world as her child. Now as Anjana, the daughter of the monkey chief Kundala and the wife of Kesari, she was attempting to complete what she had left incomplete in her previous birth. In this life, she hadn’t allowed a single wrong desire or thought to pass through her mind. Days turned into weeks, weeks into months. Anjana’s intense devotion only increased. She lost track of time and became absorbed in some higher deeper realm.

This continued for a while. Until one day, when she felt something hair-raising in her vicinity!

Although she had no fear, she found this strange. She felt someone holding her. When she opened her eyes, she saw nothing but an empty cave. She closed her eyes yet again to refocus. The same eerie feeling engulfed her again. Someone was surely playing a prank on her and testing her patience. When she opened her eyes this time, they were balls of fire. She flew into a fit of rage and screamed into the emptiness of the cave, “Whoever you are, stop fooling around, else I will put a curse on you!”

No sooner had she pronounced her verdict, than a lustrous being dressed in flowing white garments appeared before her. “Vayu, the wind god,” Anjana whispered under her breath, instantly recognizing the divine entity. “Greetings, O lord of the wind. Please pardon me for my angry outburst. I did not know it was you. May I request the reason for your visit, my lord?” Vayu accepted Anjana’s apology and in his trademark swishing voice said, “I would have to narrate two stories to explain the reason.” He saw Anjana’s ears perk up. Noticing her eagerness and curiosity, Vayu launched into the tales he had come to share.

eleventh rudra

Thousands of followers sat around him, waiting for him to open his eyes. None knew how long the wait would be. No one wanted to be absent when he did open his eyes. He would enter such a state many times, and the experience would be different each time not only because of how long it took, but also how he came out of the trance. There was always a deep mystery associated with his waking up – a secret known only to him.

“Rama…Rama…Rama…”

Everyone present – his closest associates included – was surprised by the awakening words. Who was Rama? Everyone was curious. His eyes were still shut, so there were still some moments left to admire this hero.

Always seated in the flawless lotus posture, this best of the yogis was the embodiment of silent, absolute power. Tiger skin draped his perfectly chiselled body; a peacefully shut vertical third eye with three neat horizontal lines of white ash running across it decorated his wide forehead; bands of rudraksha beads encircled his strong biceps, wrists and matted hair top knot that held in within it a crescent moon and the flowing Ganga. Lord Shiva was the perfect abode of peace and tranquillity.

When he finally opened his eyes, he found his wife, the gorgeous Sati, by his side. Her sixth sense had told her that he needed her presence. Seeing her by his side, Lord Shiva lovingly spoke into her ears. “Lord Vishnu is incarnating soon on earth to annihilate the demonic forces accumulated by Ravana and to end his atrocities. He has instructed all demigods to be born as monkeys to assist Him in His lila. I don’t want to miss this golden chance to be associated with His avatar as Lord Rama. I too will transform into a monkey to participate in this Rama lila.”

Sati was intrigued that of all avatars, he chose that of a monkey to serve Rama. “Why have you chosen to be a monkey?” she asked. Shiva replied, “Monkeys are completely detached and can do anything without shame. In my service to the lord, I want no obstructions, be it high birth, vain beauty, high education, great fame or immense opulence.”58

The answer upset Sati. It seemed as if she would never really experience the bliss of togetherness with her husband. While he was with her, he was meditating, and when he returned to consciousness, he was serving. Not wanting to stop her husband’s desire to serve Lord Rama, she made a unique request. “My dear lord, if you plan to transform into a monkey for this divine task, may I accompany you in your service by transforming into your tail? The tail is the ornament of a monkey. So let me become your tail imbued with all your divine potencies. If, as shaktimaan, you assume a monkey form, then let me, your shakti, become your tail.”59

Shiva was more than happy to oblige. But Sati still had a doubt. “How could you offer so many boons to Ravana and seem to be his well-wisher on the one hand, and plot his destruction on the other?” she asked. Shiva smiled at her innocence and inquisitiveness. “Although Ravana has managed to please my ten avatars or rudras by offering his ten heads, he has dissatisfied and angered my eleventh avatar. My eleventh rudra will now incarnate on earth as the son of Anjana and will be known as Hanuman.”

potent slip-up

Everyone in the universe was talking about her! Where did she come from? Where did she go? Who was she? Why hadn’t anyone seen her ever before? Why was everyone so mesmerized by her charms?

So many questions, yet no answers.

All they knew about her was that her name was Mohini. In fact, nothing else mattered to those men ogling at her. Her name enhanced her charm.

Of course everything was resolved the moment Mohini lifted the sudarshan chakra and severed the head of a demon named Rahu. Everyone was aghast to discover that Mohini was none other than Lord Vishnu himself. Lord Vishnu had taken many incarnations in the past, but no one had ever expected him to become an enchantress.60

The moment Lord Shiva heard of Lord Vishnu’s latest avatar, he was very eager to see the new form. He was unfortunately absent when it happened. So he approached Lord Vishnu and requested him to transform in the enchanting form even if for a second. Lord Vishnu refused repeatedly. “No, I will not become her again. I had chosen the avatar for a purpose. Now that the purpose has been served, I don’t see why I must become her unless there is another pressing reason. I wouldn’t want to become Mohini again.”

But Lord Shiva insisted, pleaded and begged for a moment’s darshan of the enchanting Mohini. Lord Vishnu was still very hesitant. He began to warn Lord Shiva of the possibility of a lot of embarrassment if he were to become Mohini again. Shiva couldn’t care less and was adamant. After several warnings and refusals, Lord Vishnu relented.61

Lo and behold, this was Mohini murti in all her glory!

One look at Mohini’s mind-boggling beauty drove Lord Shiva crazy. He began to ogle at her and completely lost sight of who was around him. He even lost memory of who he was and how important a role he had in the universal creation. All that mattered to him right now was Mohini. He slowly began to advance toward her.

In that moment, he forgot that he was the husband of the illusory potency of the Lord, Durga or Mayashakti. Here he was, bewitched by the lord’s illusory potency in full action.

Seeing Lord Shiva advance toward her, Mohini began to slip away. Shiva chased after her in great desperation with arms outstretched. Mohini began running away with a big smile on her face, teasing Shiva. The faster she ran, the greater the intensity in his steps. Suddenly, she manifested a ball in her hand and began to bounce it around while continuing to move away. This exasperated Shiva even more. She didn’t even seem to be putting any effort into running, yet she was out of Shiva’s reach.

Mohini now did something unbelievable. She began running toward the hermitages where Shiva’s worshippers were gathered, chanting his name fervently. These sadhus and sanyasis were intense and followed strict vows of celibacy and abstinence to please Lord Shiva through their austerities. When, from the corner of their eyes, they saw the silhouette of this beautiful damsel playing with a ball pass by their ashram, they all lowered their heads to avoid her. They didn’t want to displease their master Shiva by pleasuring their senses.

As she passed by like a swift breeze, they twisted uncomfortably in their seats. Then they saw the most horrifying sight of all: their master chasing the beautiful damsel – their master Lord Shiva. This could not be true. The supreme yogi who had conquered lust could not possibly be chasing after this beautiful embodiment of desire. They had no idea what to do now. They had no idea what to think even. What was expected of them?62

Unmindful of his followers’ questioning eyes, Shiva followed Mohini, immersed in her gorgeous splendor. He had dropped all control and had allowed himself to be towed by Mohini’s beauty-leash in any direction she chose. After hours and hours of teasing, Mohini allowed Lord Shiva to touch her just once. As soon as they touched, he released his potency and handed over that energetic conception to Vayu to deliver it to Anjana at the appropriate moment.

Vayu was unsure of his ability to handle the immense power given to him for safekeeping, because Shiva’s seed was so hot that if it were in his possession for too long, the winds would always be hot and the world would perish.

Just in time, the sapta rishis – the seven powerful celestial sages – came to the rescue. They had brought along with them a metal leaf from the Mandakini Ganga for Vayu to place the potent seed in it. “We assure you of its safe custody with us until you come to reclaim it, Vayu.”

Lessons from Lord Shiva and Mohini

This story highlights the power of the illusory energy of Lord Vishnu. If Lord Shiva can succumb to it, then it’s so much more likely for a normal living entity to fall under the trap of illusion. Thus one has to learn to deal with it carefully.

Though humiliated in front of all his followers, Lord Shiva was humble and unaffected by the embarrassment. He knew very well that Lord Vishnu was using him as an example to warn so many people who venture out on the path to perfection to be careful while dealing with the Lord’s maya shakti or illusory energy.

It also teaches us to take warnings given to us seriously and not only focus on the immediate pleasure that lies ahead of us.

vision realized

Her clairvoyance told her exactly why she was experiencing those extraordinary emotions today amid her meditation on the topmost cliff of a rocky mountain. This was the day she had waited for all her life. Her joy revolved around the joys of her son, Hanuman, who was born when Vayu offered her the seed of Lord Shiva through her ear.

Even before he was born, she was told about this day. She had been waiting for decades for this day to come. But her joy today was incomparable to what she had been anticipating all these days.

Today her son had met his master!

His life’s greatest adventure was just beginning. Prostrating herself mentally in front of the savior, the master, the guide who had come to claim her son, Anjana shed profuse tears of joy.

Today, her role as a mother had been fulfilled. Her son had achieved perfection of his existence by getting to serve and connect with such a divine master.

Anjana was now a happy and satisfied mother. She silently blessed her son with her heart’s profuse wishes.63

thread of hope

Hanuman had noticed Sugriva’s combined expressions of fear and negative self-esteem. He backed his own decision because he had his reasons. I was right.When the brothers asked me why Sugriva could not go to them and why they had to go to him instead, I was right in telling them that a regular patient visits the doctor but when the patient is too sick, the doctor has to make the visit. Sugriva is so tired of running away from his fears that he now has no more strength left to even walk toward his hope.64

After dropping off Rama and Lakshmana at the topmost cliff of the Rishimukh mountain, Hanuman again took the form of a beggarsanyasi and went to meet his master Sugriva. He knew well that Sugriva’s mind was honeycombed with doubts. He was now in the habit of suspecting every occurrence that was out of the ordinary. In fact, his mind would be plagued by mistrust if any of his associates were not to follow his instructions. Hanuman realized that if he returned to Sugriva without the disguise, then he would assume that he had done a shabby job at assessing the two men. The disguise was just to prevent Sugriva’s mind from going into overdrive.65

“The one you feared as a problem is actually the solution you have been seeking.” Hanuman uttered these words of advice to Sugriva with folded hands. “The two personalities are the celebrated descendants from the great house of the Ikshvaku kings. They are the sons of the great king Dasaratha who is accredited with performing several glorious yajnas such as the Ashwamedha and Rajasuya. Just hearing their names – Rama and Lakshmana – brings hope to the ears. They are worthy of praise and honor. I would highly recommend that you befriend them for your own benefit.66

“Let me assure you that Rama is dharma personified. Worship him with great veneration. I cannot explain to you the kind of emotional surcharge I experienced the moment my eyes rested on Rama. Every bone in my body began to melt as a mark of great love toward Rama. For someone with indigestion, feasting is not advisable, but Rama is both a feast and a medicine. Rama initially acts as a medicine for those who have indigestion, and when one is cured, He becomes a feast. Rama does not merely speak of dharma, but He also practices it. Rama not only has the desire to protect those who seek His shelter, but He also has the ability to safeguard them.

“O Sugriva, cast off your ape-like form right away and assume a human form to greet these divine princes. Abandon your forlorn expression and don the expression of hope to welcome prosperity into your life.”67

Hanuman’s words always stirred something in Sugriva’s heart. No matter how downcast he was, he would seek out hope from some deep corner of his heart the moment Hanuman began speaking. Surgiva stood up once again on the strength of borrowed hope!

brothers in misery

Just as the brothers were beginning to wonder where the monkey had disappeared leaving them stranded on the mountain peak, they noticed something that made them smile.

Standing in front of them were two contradictory personalities. While one was the beggar-sanyasi they had met earlier, the other was a handsome, princely human being dressed up for the occasion. One was Hanuman, and the other had to be Sugriva. But wasn’t he supposed to be a wonder-monkey, too? Why is he disguised as a human? The brothers smiled when they simultaneously realized that Sugriva was uncomfortable with himself. They realized that he gave too much importance to the outside than the inside of a personality.68

The odd duo began to walk toward the smiling brothers. In their hands were royal paraphernalia to welcome their guests: sandalwood paste, fresh water, fragrant flowers and soft cloth placed neatly on a big silver platter. After keeping the items on a flat rock close by, Sugriva walked up to the brothers with folded hands.

“It is an honor that a prince from the exalted Ikshvaku clan has chosen to befriend a monkey like me. Hanuman has told me about all your divine attributes.” Extending one arm toward Rama, he continued, “Kindly accept my hand as a genuine extension of my friendship and commitment toward you. If you desire my friendship in spite of knowing that I am a monkey, kindly take my arm.”69

The next instant Rama held out His hand and grabbed Sugriva’s. An unfathomable thrill passed through Sugriva the instant he felt Rama’s touch. But he was hardly prepared for what happened next. Rama gently tugged at his hand and pulled him into His loving embrace. Holding Sugriva tightly, Rama smiled. Tears flowed profusely from Sugriva’s eyes. Here was a stranger he had just met showering him with so much love. Quite unlike the blood brother he had known forever handing him only hate. Genuine friendship, he concluded, could indeed be thicker than blood.

Rama pulled Himself away from the embrace, held Sugriva at an arm’s length and stared into his eyes with genuineness. For a few moments, Sugriva was unable to gaze directly into Rama’s eyes. After the initial hesitation, he finally met His stare. What he saw in Rama’s eyes filled his own with tears of joy. All he could see was pure compassion – the kind of love unaffected by the boundaries of species, caste, position, status, age, whatsoever. The two newfound friends stared at each other for a long time.70

When Sugriva heard Rama speaking for the first time, he found His words mesmerizing and his voice captivatingly endearing. “Sugriva, my friend, destiny has brought us together in an unbelievable way. We are brothers in misery. I too am facing every misery you are going through. It’s unbelievable how two unconnected, unrelated people can face the exact same circumstance in their lives. Friendship born out of the spirit of enjoyment wanes with time, but friendship forged in the womb of misery lasts a lifetime. Sugriva, we are bonded together forever in this incredible friendship.”71

As the two friends were busy connecting with one another, the beggar-sanyasi reverted to his own self as Hanuman. Realizing the momentousness of the occasion, Hanuman contemplated the impact of this friendship on the world. He also realized that Sugriva’s unstable and flickering mind needed a stabilizing factor to hold on to this friendship. As soon as this thought hit him, Hanuman began to run around collecting things and arranging them in an order. Very soon, a beautiful yajna kunda was created with flowers and fruits neatly arranged around it. Hanuman lit the sacred fire and officiated a ceremony to seal this divine friendship.

Clasping each other’s hands, the two new friends went around the fire seven times, taking oaths of unbreakable commitments toward this friendship. Holding their right hand high over the sacred fire, Rama and Sugriva spoke in unison, “Sealed by this sacred presence of Agni, the fire god, I commit to this bond of friendship. From today onwards, my friend will be your friend, and your enemy will be my enemy; my happiness will be your happiness, and your suffering will be my suffering.”

Sugriva added to the commitment. “I will bring back Your Sita from wherever She is, just as Vishnu brought back the stolen Vedas.”

“Whether your enemies are coursing the skies, walking the earth or hiding anywhere else, I commit to destroy them all.” Rama assured him.72

twitching eyes

Nila, Nala and Taru were impressed with their master’s humility. For the first time, they saw him serve someone other than his brother. They realized Sugriva must really be impressed with Rama to serve him like this. While the three of them were staring at their master in awe, Hanuman was looking at him with disgust. “How could he do this?” Hanuman was aghast at what had just happened.

When the three of them saw Hanuman’s contorted face, they knew that something was wrong with their master’s actions. What had impressed them had clearly disappointed Hanuman. In trying to understand what had gone wrong, they tried to recall all that had just transpired between Rama and Sugriva.

As soon as the fire ritual was complete and the two friends Rama and Sugriva had taken their vows of commitment, the three thus-far-hidden wonder-monkeys Nila, Nala and Taru made their appearance and greeted Rama and Lakshmana. While Hanuman was busy completing the formalities of the ritual fire yajna, Sugriva disappeared into the nearby thicket. In a few minutes, he was back with a huge flowery branch of a sala tree. Placing it carefully at the center of the flat cliff, with a gesture of his hand, Sugriva welcomed Rama to take his seat. Rama sat on one corner of the branch allowing ample space on the same branch for his brother Lakshmana. Even before Lakshmana could take a step toward the branch to occupy the seat, Sugriva went ahead and occupied the empty space himself.

Hanuman winced at the foolishness of his master. Even as the three wonder-monkeys focused on Sugriva’s desire to serve Rama, Hanuman noticed his offence toward Lakshmana. While the three were appreciative of his good intention of serving Rama, Hanuman was condemning his mistake of neglecting Lakshmana.73

For a split second, Hanuman thought of directly telling Sugriva about his mistake. Then he stopped himself, knowing that it wasn’t the right etiquette to point out a master’s mistake in front of others and that the best way to correct a superior was by doing the correct thing and leading by example. Hoping Sugriva would understand and learn a lesson for the future, Hanuman went into the thicket and returned with a branch of a better tree. He quietly placed the branch of the fragrant sandalwood tree next to the sala branch Rama and Sugriva were seated on. Hanuman stood aside and invited Lakshmana to take his seat.

When Lakshmana occupied his seat, Hanuman did something that opened everyone’s eyes to good etiquette. Instead of sitting alongside Lakshmana, he chose to sit on the floor next to Lakshmana’s feet. As soon as he did that, a subtle smile appeared on the lotus face of Lord Rama. Recognition at last!

Although Hanuman’s Supreme Master had acknowledged his gesture, his chief Sugriva was so preoccupied with conversing with Rama that he failed to notice, let alone understand his mistake. Sugriva’s disrespect toward Lakshmana had disturbed Rama, but Hanuman’s honorable act impressed Him. Sugriva’s assistants now began to look the incident in a new light. Although initially impressed with their master’s servitude, now Hanuman’s sensitivity stirred them. What intrigued them, however, was the look on Rama’s face. Did they notice a subtle smile for Hanuman and dagger eyes for Sugriva?74

Rama’s emotion flickered for only a fraction of a second, so Sugriva’s assistants could not be sure. Before they could make sense of it, Rama was back to His normal smiling self, listening intently to Sugriva’s tale of woe. As Sugriva began expressing his life’s complexities, he broke down and begged Rama to help him – now that they were friends. When he began explaining that the main attribute of true friendship was the willingness to do something for a friend who can’t do it by himself, Rama rose abruptly, interrupting his flow.

“I, the son of Dasaratha, the descendent of the glorious Ikshvaku dynasty vow that I will annihilate your enemy very soon.” Rama declared with great anger and determination in His eyes.

Three eyes began to twitch simultaneously. The moment Rama took His vow, a single phenomenon occurred in three different corners of the world.

A flower from the Ashoka tree fell at Sita’s feet. As soon as She picked up that flower, something happened: Her lotus-like left eye began to twitch uncontrollably. She began to smile to the amazement of hundreds of rakshasis around Her. They wondered why Her left eye twitched so much. They were even more baffled by Her constant smile because this had happened for the first time since Sita was held captive. Between them, they couldn’t agree about what made Her smile, but they all agreed about the brilliance of Her smile.

In the amorous embrace of his wife, the most beautiful Tara, Vali found his greatest joy in life. He loved her company in every possible way. Just as he was about to say something sweet to make her happy, something happened. His green-colored left eye began to twitch uncontrollably. He had no idea what had happened suddenly. When Tara saw her husband’s left eye twitch, she began to perspire. She became speechless. She turned morbidly cold as if death itself had knocked at her door.

Ravana loved practice matches because they combined his love for weapons, fights and watching his opponents bleed. Just as he was about to strike the fatal blow with his sword held high in the air and his legs leaping high above the ground, something happened. Ravana’s fiery red left eye began to twitch relentlessly. The twitching was so bad that Ravana missed his mark and landed with a thud on the floor. His opponent took advantage of Ravana’s loss of balance and slashed his sword, causing a neat cut on Ravana’s left arm. Ravana flew into a fit of rage. Forgetting for a moment that this was a friendly match, Ravana decapitated the man with one swish of his sword. The eyelid fluttering continued, much to the demon’s dismay.

The three simultaneously wondered about the implication of the twitching eyes on their lives. Three lives were now interconnected by one vow – the first step heralding irreversible change in their lives had just been taken.75

right choice

Sugriva and his associates had gone into their caves to fetch something the exiled monkey chief had wanted Rama to see. He had mentioned that it was an important proof of abduction. This gave Rama and Lakshmana some private time with each other. They took this breather to discuss the exciting yet bewildering events of the day.

Lakshmana shifted along his tree-branch seat to come closer to his brother. He spoke his mind, “I am still trying to decipher the word vanara. After meeting these wonder-monkeys, do you understand what the word really means?”

Instead of answering him directly, Rama smiled and said something that seemed completely disconnected to his question. Lakshmana wondered if Rama had heard him at all, but he knew better than to correct Him. Often Rama’s indirect answers eventually provided deeper insight about the question. Lakshmana decided to simply listen attentively and wait for clarity to dawn on him.

“Shabari and Kabandha were right in recommending this alliance with Sugriva rather than Vali, which would have been the more obvious and easy choice. Considering that Vali’s strength and enterprising abilities are exemplary, he would have made a better and a natural preference, but Sugriva has four strengths that make him our ideal partner at this point of time.

“The first is that Sugriva’s greatest strength is his minister Hanuman. That one association makes him stronger than the strongest. The second is Sugriva’s simplicity. Only a person ready to admit his shortcomings and weaknesses can be helped. Unless a patient admits to a doctor the manner of his suffering, no matter how embarrassing the symptom, he cannot be helped. By sharing his weaknesses with us, Sugriva has exhibited his simplicity. The third strength is the fact that he is a spark of the sun god. Because the sun governs him, he has the knack of deriving inspiration from others. Yet the fourth and the greatest strength is that he has been thoroughly cleansed by time. Because he has gone through so many traumas in his life, he has been humbled and purified. He is now in the best stage to offer help because he himself has experienced helplessness. Although disrespectful and insensitive, he has the right attitude. Besides, all his drawbacks can be easily rectified.76

“Vali on the other hand, although formidable has four weaknesses that disqualify him from achieving anything good in life. His first weakness is that he is a spark of Indra, and that makes him dominating. Autocracy is the greatest weakness of a leader. While the dominating tendency helps a leader keep others under his subjugation, it doesn’t inspire others to follow him. His second weakness is his craze for sensuality that blinds him from seeing clearly. Dissatisfied with his own wife, his blind lust has led him to abduct his own brother’s wife. How could someone who has committed the same crime as Ravana be expected to help Me save Sita? His third weakness is that unlike Sugriva, time hasn’t purified him yet. His pride and arrogance are at their peak, and he considers himself infallible. This overconfidence and haughtiness will lead him to his death. Nevertheless, the fourth and the greatest weakness of all is that he is cursed by a saint. Anyone cursed by a saint has already lost half his powers. That is precisely the main reason Shabari and Kabandha recommended us to align with Sugriva and not Vali. The mind and luck of someone cursed by a saint are unreliable.77

“Most monkeys of Kishkinda follow four principles to ensure a life of full enjoyment. They believe in these principles strongly because their leader propounds it by personal example. The first is sabharyaa – many men enjoy with one woman. The second is abharya – taking another man’s wife for pleasure – so many men are without their wives. The third is lupta – insatiable greed for women. The fourth is viprayukta – driving others away from their homes and occupying their possessions and wives. Their leader Vali inspires them to follow such a lifestyle.

“In the same Kishkinda area, you will find monkeys like Hanuman at the other end of the spectrum – highly reliable, cultured, sensitive, erudite and faithful. Then there are those in between the two extremes, monkeys like Sugriva. Personalities like Sugriva mold themselves according to the company they keep. If they associate with people like Vali, they behave like lustful monkeys, and if they associate with monkeys like Hanuman, they behave like cultured humans; therefore, they are called vanara, meaning ‘are you human?’ It’s a rhetorical question that needs to be answered minute to minute. Every time they exhibit cultured behavior, they are ‘nara’ or humans, and every time they exhibit uncultured behavior like the rakshasas, they are monkeys. Since they keep asking themselves this question regularly, many monkeys prefer to take on their human form with the help of kaamarupi vidya or shape-shifting sciences. Some monkeys like Sugriva believe that focusing on transforming physically into nara is more important than adopting the qualities of nara. However, monkeys like Hanuman are convinced that form is hardly of any consequence in comparison to qualities. Thus, they struggle to exhibit superior nature by being extremely sensitive and diligent in their dealings and behavior.78 Between the untrainable Vali and the supertrained Hanuman stands the trainable Sugriva.”

“Lucid! What an explanation! Such clarity on the nature of people.” Lakshmana was impressed with Rama’s analysis. Everything made so much sense now. Rama had the knack of making even the most complex things so palatably simple.

By the time they spotted the five wonder-monkeys coming towards them, their discussion had almost ended.

divine endorsement

Rama’s hands were trembling as He opened the package. Tears flooded His eyes. The touch of the soft cloth of the wrapping felt very familiar. When he unwrapped it, his eyes were so full of tears that He could hardly see anything. On seeing his brother’s condition, Lakshmana walked up to Him and placed his arm around His shoulder. Rama turned toward Lakshmana and held the bundle in His hands so that His brother could see its contents clearly. Lakshmana glanced at the bundle in Rama hands. It seemed like tangled jewelry. Nothing was obvious. What was obvious was that the jewels were all intact; neither the shape nor the form of the jewelry seemed damaged.

Lakshmana began to wonder if he had perhaps seen the jewelry somewhere or if these could be Sita’s. Perhaps She had bundled them up and hurled them at Sugriva below while being whisked away by force in Ravana’s vimana. It was a wonder that every piece of jewelry was undamaged despite being flung from such a height. If She had intentionally thrown these, then surely this episode had serious significance. Lakshmana remembered that during the long walk from Panchavati to Kishkinda, he had noticed many hermitages along the forests. If Sita did in fact throw the jewelry, then why did She choose this particular wonder-monkey settlement and not any hermitage of some sage?

Sugriva said, “A distressed woman had thrown the bundle directly at me and not at the other monkeys. Her aim was so accurate, that this rolled up packet landed on the soft grass at my feet.” Now that explains it. Lakshmana now knew why the jewelry was intact. But something continued to baffle him. Why choose Sugriva over Hanuman? Lakshmana knew Sita was no ordinary person. She was the Divine Mother. If indeed it was Sita, then Lakshmana knew Her choice meant that Sugriva had received Her grace. It then dawned upon Lakshmana that this was the fifth reason, and reason enough, to ally with Sugriva and not Vali.79

Lakshmana inspected the jewelry. These were mostly necklaces, earrings and bracelets. Despite scrutinizing them several times, he could not recognize the items. He gave up, frustrated that he was unable to fulfill his brother’s unspoken request. He realized that no matter how much he stared at them, he would never be able to establish with certainty if they really belonged to Sita. For the simple reason that in the last thirteen years of their exile, he had perhaps seen them but never really looked to know for sure. Although Sita wore her jewelry every single day, he never ever scanned them. So how was he to verify if they belonged to Sita?

But Lakshmana also knew that Rama gave a lot of weightage to his keen sense of discrimination. Even when the golden deer came into their life, he had remained unaffected by the illusion and had had the clarity to point out that it was a fake. Perhaps after that bad experience with the fake golden deer, Rama wanted him to certify if these ornaments indeed belonged to Sita or if these too were fake. Keeping all these factors in mind, Lakshmana said something that took everyone by surprise.80

*naham janami keyure*

*naham janami kundale*

*nupurevaham janami*

*nityam padabivandanat*

“I have no idea how Her armlets look; I have no idea how Her earrings look. If you show me Her anklets, I can identify them for sure because I have never looked above Mother Sita’s lotus feet. Since I have offered millions of obeisances to her, I can clearly recognize Her anklets at first sight.”

The monkeys, especially Sugriva, gaped at him in utter shock as if he had come from another planet. What a difference between Vali and Lakshmana’s worlds! How different a monkey and human were! Sugriva realized that just taking on the human form was not enough, it was also important to adopt the essence of that form. Although vanaras were experts at imitating the form, they had a long way to go before being able to emulate the core. Here was a brother who wouldn’t look beyond his sister-in-law’s feet because he worshipped Her, and there was another who kidnapped his sister-in-law to enjoy her.

Even though His eyes were wet, Rama couldn’t help but break into a smile at his brother’s words of extreme integrity. Rama had always noticed that Lakshmana was always very careful in his interactions with Sita. In fact, throughout their thirteen years of exile, Lakshmana had not even stayed in the same house as them. He had preferred to stay some distance away, keeping a protective watch over them and at the same time not interfering in their privacy. Immediately, a thought ran through Rama’s mind. How could Sita have even doubted the character of such an exalted and good-natured brother-in-law?81

In the meantime, Hanuman had sorted out the jewels, separating the anklets from the rest. He handed them over to Sugriva, who in turn gave those to Lakshmana. “Yes, these are Hers!” Lakshmana could not contain his excitement.With this confirmation Rama’s dam of emotions burst open, flooding His face with tears. He grabbed the ornaments and clutched them tightly to His chest. He began to smell them. Surely, they carried Sita’s fragrance. His body began to heat up so much that the adornments appeared to melt to slush and smear His body. Sugriva grabbed Rama’s shoulder. His torso was burning with the venom left by the snake of separation.

“What misfortune!” Rama cried bitterly staring teary-eyed at the ornaments that once decorated Sita’s delicate body. “In spite of being the wife of the one who carries a mighty bow, Sita had to remove all the ornaments from Her body like a widow. My ancestors managed to bring even Ganga from the heavens. My father fought on behalf of the demigods. But look at their incompetent descendent. He is not even able to relieve His wife of Her misery.”

Distressed by Rama’s plight, Sugriva offered some wise words of consolation. With folded hands, he articulated his thoughts. “O Rama, I am not competent enough to advice you, but as a true friend I would like to offer you the benefit of my experiences through words. I am a monkey, but I haven’t lost courage despite losing my wife and kingdom to my evil brother. For a person of Your caliber and status, such despair is unbecoming. Amid the greatest of adversities, losses and dangers, a wise man retains his cool and deals with challenges with intense focus and intelligence. A man with an average mind succumbs to grief just like an overloaded ship wobbles in the sea.”82

Rama weighed Sugriva’s words and gathered Himself. He then asked His new friend a befuddling question. “Where do you think Ravana has taken Sita? Which direction did he fly toward?”

Sugriva was stumped, but a thought crossed his mind. If I reveal Ravana’s exact location right away, then a surge of great anger may make Rama venture in that direction immediately, without relieving me of my burden. Sugriva realized that he had to mold his answer to protect his interests. “I have no knowledge of the whereabouts of Ravana’s abode.” Sugriva lied.83

Hanuman then shared his thoughts with the brothers. “O Lords, I humbly implore you to pay attention to my suggestion. It may give you some clarity about your future course of action. We are yet to know where Ravana has taken Mother Sita. He took Her away in a flying machine. He could potentially have kept Mother Sita captive in any corner of the globe. Proceeding in one direction and moving from place to place will serve no purpose. With each step you take closer to Her, he may keep shifting Her farther and farther away. This chase is definitely going to prove useless.

“Instead, if you help Sugriva eliminate Vali, you will facilitate his reascension to the throne and help him become the undisputed king of the stupendous monkey army. With this, you will automatically have millions of monkeys at your disposal to comb the entire planet for Mother Sita. Nothing is impossible for such a vast, well-trained army of monkeys to accomplish.”

Rama nodded in agreement and kissed Sita’s ornaments. He was still clutching on to them tightly, but a look of fierce determination had replaced the helpless tears.

“Tell me everything you know about Vali – everything about his atrocities toward you. Tell me everything I need to know!” Rama’s resolve shone as brilliantly as the ornaments in his hands.

Rama’s questions were directed at Sugriva. No one else was expected to answer him. There was a dark aspect about Vali that only Hanuman knew. Yet he didn’t speak because he wasn’t asked to. But that didn’t mean he couldn’t think about it…84

death defeated

One king entered stealthily into another’s palace in the dead of the night. This was not a regular occurrence. But when it did happen, it meant something was going to go wrong. This was such a crucial task, that he didn’t want to risk appointing anyone else.

As he walked down the secret tunnel below the palace, Vali, the king of a section of the vanara clan, was thinking of all the events that had led up to this bold and rather risky decision.

It all began when Narada muni, the transcendental spaceman descended upon the lanes of Kishkinda. People were awestruck by his charisma. He was effulgent and extremely handsome. Singing in his divinely melodious voice and playing the chords of his famous veena, he danced his way toward the palace. King Vali greeted Narada respectfully, knowing well that he had a message to convey. If he had chosen to come all the way, then that meant that the message was of utmost importance to Vali.

After impatiently going through the reception formalities, Vali sat next to Narada to hear him out. Finally, Narada revealed the secret. A secret that shook Vali to the core, and in an instant, converted him from a confident king to an insecure monkey. In the womb of Anjana, the wife of King Kesari, an expansion of Lord Shiva was to be born. Rumors were rife in the heavenly realms that this child would be the greatest hero on earth, and his strength would surpass Vali by a thousandfold and more. Vali panicked at first, but as he thought deeply, he realized that a danger when quelled was no danger at all. He only had to act intelligently.85

Here, he was walking within Kesari’s palace walls in the dead of the night. Although Kesari was a chief in Vali’s father Riksharaja’s army, he was now the ruler of a small kingdom. He was a subordinate king to Vali, but he was a king nonetheless. Using his negotiation skills, Vali had gained access to the royal kitchen. Someone was waiting for him there – the pregnant queen’s personal cook. Kesari had appointed her to cater to the diet restrictions of the queen. This woman was an expert at cooking for expectant mothers. For the next meal, however, she was going to serve the queen something not recommended for any trimester – something fatally dangerous. She would serve the queen a special concoction Vali had given her!

The next morning, the cook carefully heated the liquid Vali had given her the night before. She could clearly sniff the metals gold, copper, iron, tin and zinc in it, but it was made with such ingenuity that it was impossible to detect the ingredients. All Vali had told her was that this potion would do its job. But she wasn’t just anyone; she knew exactly what it contained. As she carried the glass containing the piping hot liquid, her hands shook. She knew it was wrong; it was terribly wrong to have a heavily pregnant woman drink anything comprising five metals. One of these could kill not only the child, but also the mother.

She closed her eyes, not wanting to watch Anjana gulp down what she believed was a health drink for her and her baby. As she took the glass back from the queen, she searched her face for reactions, perhaps a rash or a boil. No, nothing? Surprising! The complete absence of any side effect shocked the cook. But she decided to wait for a full minute, just in case any effect takes longer to appear. Nothing! Not knowing what to do, she just walked back to the kitchen. She kept pacing anxiously back and forth between the kitchen and the queen’s room, desperately waiting to see some change. Even after hours, nothing happened to the queen, just nothing at all.

The next day, Anjana’s spasms started, and she was rushed to the labor room. Vali’s aide, the cook, waited outside in anticipation of news of a miscarriage. Instead, the cry of a newborn greeted her. As everyone celebrated, she sank to her knees. How was it possible for a woman to deliver a perfectly normal baby after having consumed a five-metal amalgam? She collected herself after a while and ran into the room to see the baby who had defeated death. When she saw the child’s face, she fainted.

The monkey baby was smiling at her. The baby was born with earrings made from the alloy of those five metals! Instead of destroying him, the fatal concoction ended up decorating him.86

pumped out

Years passed, but not the vengeance in Vali’s mind. He was just as insecure as he was on the day of Hanuman’s birth. His hatred toward Hanuman increased when he joined hands with Sugriva, his greatest enemy. Hanuman was only supposed to be Sugriva’s minister, but he soon quickly became everything to him. Initially, Vali only had to monitor his brother’s movements and wait for the right time: the moment Sugriva stepped out of the precincts of the Rishimukh mountain, Vali struck. But from the day Hanuman stalled him just when he was about to pummel his brother with his patent flying kick, Vali realized that this powerful monkey warrior was a greater threat to his happy existence.

From that day forth, Vali kept a special spy force to monitor the movements of Hanuman too. In fact, the more news he gathered about Hanuman’s abilities, adventures and unique strengths, the more he was convinced about eliminating him rather than Sugriva. The way he saw it, Sugriva was a weakling compared to Hanuman. In a way, although he had to be eliminated, he wasn’t really a threat to Vali’s life. Hanuman on the other hand, was as yet his biggest menace.

Vali’s patience paid off when the opportunity arrived unsought. He jumped off his throne the moment he got the news from his spies. He dashed into his private chambers. Tara was very surprised to see her husband enter the private chambers at this time of the day. She was too intelligent to not understand its implication. Only two things in that room were invaluable for Vali – her and the ornate necklace. Sometimes it made her wonder which of the two was dearer to her husband. Vali rushed toward the safe that had the jewel case and put on that special necklace. Tara’s heart sank. It meant that she had lost to a piece of jewelry yet again and that Vali was getting ready for war against a formidable enemy.

To fight an enemy he considered weak, Vali wouldn’t bother putting on his necklace. But this time, he not only wore the necklace, but he also had a strange look on his face. As Vali made his way out of the room, Tara sensed that this enemy was not only powerful, but also probably the most challenging one he had ever faced. She offered a silent prayer for his protection. Although she knew that her husband considered himself invincible as long as he had that necklace, she had her reservations. She was convinced that no one was invincible and nothing was foolproof. As a wife, she wanted to warn him desperately, but as someone with experience, she knew of its futility. She walked to her balcony and stood silently. As her eyes followed her husband as he stormed out of the palace gates, her lips murmured silent prayers.87

Vali reached the designated spot His informant had told him about. No one seemed to be around. Could his most trustworthy spies be wrong this time? It was unlikely, considering their exemplary track record. They couldn’t have made such a gross error, because they had monitored Hanuman’s habits long enough to predict his whereabouts at any time of the day. They had told him of Hanuman’s specific time and place for meditation. He preferred this very spot overlooking the valley and some distance away from the Rishimukh mountains because it provided complete privacy and the perfect environment to focus. Hanuman spent all day serving Sugriva, but he needed this time to feel his connect with the Supreme – his real source of strength.

Vali decided to wait patiently in the hope of accomplishing a longpending goal today. After a prolonged delay, just when he was about to end his stakeout, he heard the sound of footsteps crushing leaves. He tightened his muscles, clenched his fists and readied himself for a tough fight.

Unaware of being watched, Hanuman walked toward his favorite destination. Seclusion at last! Nothing mattered to him as much as the solace in this place. He always yearned for these private moments – moments he could surrender himself to the sublime and be absorbed in the higher and deeper aspects of life. Although born a warrior par excellence, Hanuman always considered himself a thinker and a worshipper.88

As he stood with his hands on the hips, he looked down at the valley below him and admired its breathtaking beauty. Hanuman considered appreciating the natural beauty of the surroundings as a great way of inspiring focus into the beauty within.

He sat down facing the beautiful valley, his back erect in a traditional yogic posture. As he slowly closed his eyes, he began to focus on the divine form of the lord within his heart. It wouldn’t have taken him too long to enter that deep meditative phase in which the world outside no longer matters, but then a crunching sound interrupted his incantation. Instantly, his warrior instincts took over, and he evaded a forceful punch just in time.

When he recovered from his jump, he saw Vali, the king of Kishkinda and Sugriva’s older brother trying to assault him to even the score. Hanuman had expected this retaliation from the day he had stunned Vali by blocking his aerial attack on Sugriva. Vali had then begged Hanuman to let go of him and promised never to strike Sugriva again. But Hanuman knew better than to trust Vali. He knew well that the words of one who had no control over his senses were hollow. He was certain Vali would plan something soon, but he hadn’t anticipated being challenged in his secret prayer spot.

Hanuman gathered his strength and prepared for the fight. Vali ran toward him and suddenly jumped up in the air with his fist clenched to deliver a fatal blow on Hanuman’s head. Hanuman stepped aside just in time to render Vali’s blow futile. Vali recovered instantly. He spun on his heels and landed a solid kick on Hanuman’s chest. Vali had barely noticed Hanuman stagger when he found himself on the floor writhing in pain, blood flowing out of his ears. He could not wrap his mind around the speed with which Hanuman recovered from his blow and delivered a tight slap to his left ear.

The pain notwithstanding, Vali stood up and shook himself to alertness. He charged at Hanuman and hurled himself at him, landing heavily with both his feet on Hanuman’s shoulders. Hanuman didn’t budge even an inch despite Vali putting all his power into his kick. After several failed attempts at trying to harm Hanuman with a series of kicks, punches, blows and slaps, Vali realized that his opponent was far too strong and agile. Hanuman hadn’t moved an inch from his position. Just by standing erect, with complete focus, he was able to tackle Vali’s attempts with the slightest movements of his hands and legs. Finally, Hanuman delivered a very powerful kick to Vali’s stomach, flinging Vali to a considerable distance, crashing him into a huge tree. The impact shook every leaf and every fruit off the tree, leaving it completely naked.

Vali staggered to his feet, dazed by the kick. In his mind, he assessed his situation. I can’t defeat Hanuman with my regular strength. The demigods’ blessings have made this monkey practically invincible and much stronger than I. I have to use the power of this necklace. Vali closed his eyes, grabbed the pendant with his right hand and pointed it out toward Hanuman. He began to murmur the incantation his father Indra had taught him. “Use this necklace discreetly and with a great sense of discrimination,” Indra had warned him. He had advised that Vali use it only if he were to come across a formidable enemy who could not be defeated in a normal manner. Well, Hanuman was that formidable enemy now, and it was the right time to use the power of this jewel.

As Vali chanted the mantras invoking the potency of the necklace, energy sparks began to activate the ornament’s specialties. It could suck away half the energy of the opponent and transfer it to the owner. As the pendant began doing its miracle, Vali began to feel good and powerful. In fact, his body began to swell, his muscles expanded and he felt a surge of energy enter his body. Vali flashed a grin. He could foresee Hanuman’s immediate collapse.

Wait! Why is Hanuman smiling? The smile confused Vali and his grin began to fade, as the fog dissipates with the rising sun. Hands on his waist, Hanuman looked composed and content. Even as Vali tried to make sense of that smug smile, he felt a searing pain in his chest. Something was going wrong. At first, he thought that his pendant had malfunctioned. But he soon realized that the jewel was just fine, but he wasn’t. Vali was ill equipped to handle Hanuman’s strength. The pendant’s powers could draw out and transfer half of the opponent’s energy, but Vali’s system began to crumble after absorbing only a tiny fraction of it. Vali realized that there was only so much energy his body could contain. The surplus was destroying him. His body began to collapse and shut down and his head started reeling, making him feel like the earth was on a crazy fast spin. Every molecule in Vali’s body began to vibrate vigorously until he fell to the ground.89

With the smile still on his face, Hanuman walked away to yet another secluded spot to continue what he came to do. Eventually, Vali’s spies found their master lying unconscious under a naked tree. They carried him back home. No one ever spoke about this episode in Kishkinda. It was a forgotten one because it was an embarrassing one. Physically, Vali recovered in a few days, but mentally it took him a whole lot longer. Historians deleted those few days from Vali’s life, and he continued to rule as the invincible superhero and wonder-monkey of Kishkinda.90

The memories of this incident continued to traverse the lanes of Hanuman’s fertile mind, but he chose to neither focus on it nor brag about it. But he knew that it was time for Vali to pay for his deeds – the atrocities he had committed on the thousands of innocent people of Kishkinda. Although he had the chance to punish Vali, he had chosen not to. He had let him walk free twice in the past. On both occasions, he knew that destiny would deal with him in the way ordained by the divine will.

As Hanuman looked at Rama, he could sense that Rama’s determination to bring Vali closer to his final destiny was that divine will. He smiled, happy in the knowledge that he was part of the divine will that was to orchestrate itself soon.

## Chapter 3

Valorous Vandal

power player

There never was a hero like Vali, and there never will be. Valor, heroism, strength, courage, victory, fear – all words created to describe him.

His back was as rugged as a rocky mountain, like Kurmadev’s. His chest had the power of a thunderbolt, like Varahadev’s. His nails were razor sharp, like that of Narasingadev’s. Every element of nature seemed to have manifested itself entirely in his personality.

Clouds didn’t dare drop lightning over his home; lions didn’t dare roar from their caves in his vicinity; even raging winds didn’t dare blow hard, lest a single leaf fall on him and trigger his anger bouts. With a robust body, rippling muscles, admirable shape, humongous thighs and a voracious appetite for fighting, he was a generational classic among the warrior clan.

He tested his strength by doing unbelievably wild things. He nimbly climbed up the highest peaks and dangerously played with massive boulders, carefully maintaining his balance as a child would when playing with balls on a flat ground. He uprooted gigantic trees as if they were blades of grass, and with one whisk of his huge hand, he stripped them off all their leaves and branches.

What would take a host of demigods and hordes of demons to accomplish, he accomplished with ease and did even more all by himself! To extract nectar from the milky ocean, the demigods and demons both struggled to bear the weight of the Mandara mountain. He alone moved that incredible mountain effortlessly, thereby agitating the ocean. Although he was an army by himself, he was also the chief of an oceanic army.91

He had great inclination for battles, but he was also spiritual. He religiously followed rituals every single day. His punctuality and dedication would put even the best and the greatest to shame. No matter how pressing any matter was, he never compromised on the timings of his religious ceremonies.

He was very conscientious about his daily visit to the four directions and paying homage to the eight dikpalakas or ashta lokapalakas there. He had vowed not only to visit these eight directions every single day, but also to do so before sunrise. Every day, he woke up before brahma muhurta (or an hour and a half before sunrise) and first offered water oblations to the Ushas. Then he started his express visit with a dip in the eastern ocean for external purification. Immediately after that, he visited the southern sea to perform achamana or the ritualistic sipping of water thrice for internal purification. Next, he jumped to the western ocean to perform arghya or oblations to the directional deities. The final jump took him to the northern ocean, where he would perform archana, japa and suryopasthana or welcoming of the sun god.

Once he completed his ritualistic visits, Vali the king of the wondermonkeys leaped back in one stride, straight into his palace to prepare for another grueling day of cunning diplomacy, dirty politics and proud sovereignty!92

Mission Abandoned

In Sanskrit, Vedas are also known as shakhas and monkeys as shakha mrigas or sages who dwell among the Vedas. The monkeys in Kishkinda were originally supposed to follow the codes of dharma prescribed by the Vedas and assist Lord Rama in his adventure. But because of their close connection with Ravana, some monkeys like Vali forgot their original mission of establishing dharma and chose to focus on their self-assigned mission of self-establishment where rituals became reasons to be proud of oneself.

departed and deserted

“Both physical and intellectual powers can corrupt the greatest of minds. My life is a vivid example of that.” The exquisitely stunning Hema was sharing her thoughts with the only person she had access to. Although she was the mother of three grown children now, her beauty had remained flawless. “A father killed a father! A son killed a son! The father’s need for intellectual conquest had him killed. The son’s need for physical conquest drove him to his death. They may have achieved what they wanted for themselves, but in the process they left a wife and a mother with an empty, lonely world to deal with.”

After spending decades in this beautiful place, Hema was returning to her original home. She loved every inch of this haven, but it had now become irrelevant to her. This place had become meaningless without her husband who had painstakingly built it with his own hands. Now, with her sons dead, the place had become haunted to her. The magnificent city looked like a phantom adorned with jewels.93

Hema’s conversation with her maid, to whom she was handing over the mystical palace that she now owned, was not over. Wealth never attracted her but memories did. She wanted to leave a copy of her memories here with the person she trusted so much. She wanted to tell her everything about her mysterious life before going away forever. She wanted her thoughts to be recorded for future generations to mull over and, if possible, to learn from.94

It all began on a day considered the most important in the heavenly realms. It was the day of Indra’s victory. He had thrown a party, the scale of which was unheard of even in the heavens. This is not to say that there would never be one to undo this one. In fact, the heavens were in constant celebration amid intense competition. Each party intended to outdo the previous one. Each host aspired to be the best one yet. For invitees, these were a great source of free entertainment and networking. For those performing, these were outlets to exhibit talent.95

This was the day meant for her debut performance. The Who’s Who of the heavens were expected to grace this party. She knew this performance would make or break her career in a heaven full of dancing stars who were divine favorites.

As she stepped onto the dance floor, her eyes searched the party hall, as if looking for someone. Although she tried her best to focus on her performance and not be distracted by the powerful, attractive men in the audience, her eyes seemed to have a will of their own. As she twirled and did her award-winning step, right at the end of the back row of guests, her eyes fell on someone that not just froze but arrested her attention.

From that moment on, her entire focus was in that direction. Her feet and hands seemed to be performing exclusively for him, and she felt helplessly drawn and dragged toward him. When she eventually got a chance to see him clearly, it was evident that he was smitten by her as well. Something mystical about him held her gaze so strongly. She was so besotted by this person that she didn’t so much as glance at the other gods. It was most certainly a fatal love-at-first-sight attraction. There were so many dancers and too many luminaries in the hall, that none noticed the fragrance of an affair filling the air.

Soon Hema was in the arms of that prince charming in a lost corner in the heavens. Everything that occurred between her dancing in the hall and embracing this attractive man was erased from her memories forever. She seemed to have no recollection of how she reached this place so far away from the dance hall. All she knew was that she felt safe in his powerful arms.

The handsome prince revealed that he was the son of Kashyapa and Danu, one of the thirteen daughters of Daksha Prajapati. Hema knew that the first daughter of Daksha, Aditi, was the mother of the Adityas, the second, Diti, of the Daityas and the third, Danu, of the Danavas. He said he was the head of that Danava clan. No wonder there is such mysticism around him. The king of the Danavas has to be a magician par excellence. As if to reconfirm Hema’s thoughts, he said his name was Mayasura.

Noticing her inquisitiveness to know more about him, Mayasura said, “I have been proficient in the skill of architecture right from my childhood. To enhance this divine gift, I prayed to Lord Brahma intensely and managed to gain his favor. Lord Brahma blessed me and declared that I would become the unrivaled architect among the Devas, Danavas and Asuras.”96

Soon, he began building magnificent mansions for both Devas and Asuras, which created much hype in the three worlds. In fact, even enemies would visit each other’s palaces to see the wonders of Mayasura’s creation. He made sure there was an element of surprise and uniqueness in each of his assignments, and he repeated nothing.97

One day, he serendipitously found something that elevated his career a million notches. It was by fluke, but this simple discovery had a profound impact on his life.

He had once overheard his guru, the great sage Sukracharya, telling someone that knowledge was wealth and that it ought to be guarded just as carefully as wealth. That was it!

This one statement led Mayasura to believe in a myriad of possibilities. He knew what he was going to try was quite impossible. “So what if it has never been tried before? I will set another new trend in the world just like my architectural wonders.” With this in mind, Mayasura began his series of penances to appease Lord Brahma again. Finally, Brahma appeared before him. Mayasura promptly made his request. “Lord, please grant me the ability to gain the wealth of another by mental transference.”

“Every material thing in this world can be manipulated using the power of subtle energies.You will be able to acquire wealth by merely lying in another’s treasury and just by wishing for it.” Saying so, Lord Brahma granted him the boon.

Mayasura smiled. Lord Brahma knew there was more to this request. “I understand that you desire more than the wealth of others. What is it Mayasura?” Mayasura confided in the creator. “I wish to loot the wealth of knowledge of all architectural sciences that are in the mind of Sukracharya.” Brahma left without a comment, but with a subtle smile.98

Mayasura began exercising his power right away. Soon his mind began to flood with the kind of brilliant ideas that had never ever occurred to him. All kinds of information on architectural sciences began to pour into his mind. Within moments, Mayasura realized that he had learned and acquired more knowledge than even Vishwakarma, the celestial architect. Now no one could stop him from becoming the best in the universe. That instant, Sukracharya, who was engaged in deep meditation, realized that he had lost a part of himself. With his yogic powers, he learned of the intellectual robbery by his own disciple. It made him very upset, but he didn’t react. He knew that nature would take care of his cheating disciple. No one who had acquired knowledge by cheating his guru had ever progressed beyond a certain limit. Sukracharya decide to allow time to take care of his foolish disciple. He simply chose to keep his eyes shut to his nañve disciple’s stupidity.

Mayasura kept the robbery part hidden from Hema, of course. Who would talk about his lower nature when wooing a woman? Dramatically, when Mayasura proposed to this beautiful nymph, she blushed in acceptance. He held up his hand and gently closed the beautiful Hema’s eyes. She began to trust him implicitly and simply complied. For the few seconds her eyes were shut, Hema felt as if she was on an aerial journey in the arms of her beloved. She felt sensations that she had never felt before in her entire existence. She was in awe of her to be husband.99

Gently placing his hands over her face, he urged her to open her eyes. As she gently lifted her eyelids, her jaw dropped. “Unbelievable! Magnificent! Superlative! Spectacular! Awe-inspiring! Breathtaking! Majestic! Superb!” The adjectives kept flowing out of her mouth like a river. Hema could not believe her eyes. In spite of being born in the celestial realms where splendor and opulence was so very common, Hema was intrigued by the creation that Mayasura had displayed in front of her.

“Hemapura!” Mayapura announced, whispering into the ears of his beloved. “This city will be now known by your name forever.”

Hema had tears in her eyes. She clung on to him out of gratitude for making her feel so good within such a short time. She had never felt so much love for anyone in her life. In fact, at this moment as she lay in his arms, she felt that he was the perfect person to surrender her life to. Hemapura became the melting pot into which both of their loves liquefied and amalgamated forever.100

Years rolled by. Their love grew with time and so did Mayasura’s reputation in the world of architecture. Soon Mayasura and Hema became parents to two boys, Dundhubhi and Mayavi. In his quest to acquire intellectual prowess in the world of design, Mayasura neglected his sons and did not shower them with enough love. The boys grew to tremendous proportions and powers and became the terror for the world. While Dundhubhi managed to inspire Lord Brahma to award him the strength of ten thousand elephants, Mayavi managed to learn the art of deception to perfection from his father. But instead of using it constructively like his father, Mayavi only used it destructively like his friends, the Daityas. Both Dundhubhi and Mayavi preferred to have friends among the Daityas rather than their own clan, the Danavas. They always felt that Danavas were too docile to rule the world. Although the world so highly depended on the skills of the Danavas, they had no real recognition. The brothers felt that the Danavas were nothing but skilled laborers in the palaces of the Daityas and Devatas. With this in mind, they went out in the quest for self-establishment in the land of physical powers.101

Hema was trapped between the lives of the three men in her life. One was trying to establish himself intellectually, while the other two were fighting the world for physical supremacy. Hema knew that everything was short-lived in this world of self-establishment. So she requested her husband to give her a daughter she could raise just as she wanted. She was certain the girl would not forsake everything for self-establishment but would rather seek self-growth.102

But Hema just couldn’t conceive again. Seeing their dilemma, Mayasura commenced an intense tapasya to please Lord Shiva. When his deity appeared before him, he begged for a daughter. Lord Shiva being ashutosh, or the one easily pleased, granted the boon and said that Mayasura would very soon have a beautiful daughter but in the most unlikely manner. Leaving him puzzled, Lord Shiva disappeared. They waited, and then one day, something fantastic happened.

A frail old brahmana walked into his palace accompanied by the most beautiful girl they had ever seen. There was something special and different about the way they looked. There definitely were traces of divinity in them. The old man although so frail, looked extremely effulgent, and the young girl was so beautiful and godly.

Mayasura’s hands automatically folded in front of the old brahmana in reverence. The brahmana said, “Care for her and raise her as your own daughter. History will remember this girl as the one who will be the epitome of stability amid chaos, and she will be known as one of the five most virtuous women in the history of the universe.”

Mayasura was happy, but Hema was thrilled. Together they named her Mandodari – the one with a soft belly or udara. Knowing well that her daughter was to influence the world by her character, Hema took great pains to inculcate the highest virtues in Mandodari. Soon, this beautiful damsel became the abode of the most illustrious divine qualities. Unlike her power-hungry brothers, Mandodari preferred to dwell in a life of purity.

Time flew because it seemed as if Mandodari had walked into their lives only a few days ago when Hema and Mayasura had to watch her set out on a new phase in her life with her husband, the great Daitya king Lankeshwar Ravana. The moment Mayasura agreed to Ravana’s proposal for Mandodari, Hema knew her daughter’s would be a life of compromises. She was happy that, at least, in the short time she got to spend with Mandodari, she had prepared her for every trial that would come her way.103

Hema never met Mandodari again. But her sons had become Ravana’s close associates by virtue of being his brothers-in-law. From them she received regular news about how happy Mandodari was in Ravana’s company. She didn’t know for sure if being with Ravana was good for her daughter, but she was aware that his company was wreaking havoc in her sons’ lives. They had become even more arrogant and power hungry now. Dundubhi and Mayavi had managed to acquire astonishing powers and had grown disproportionately in body and mind.104

Although in some way chaotic, Hema’s life had settled down to a daily routine. Just when she thought that everything was back to normal, a series of disasters shook the very foundation of Hema’s life. Her life appeared so fantastic that it was the envy of all women, but in a few days, everything reversed. Her life was no longer the subject of envy. It had become a matter of universal pity.105

Just out of the blue one day, Indra visited Hemapura. What initially appeared to be a casual surprise visit in fact turned out to be a conspiracy. Indra started by appreciating Mayasura’s awe-inspiring work and commending him for his hardwork and perseverance toward changing the definition of architecture through his exemplary masterpieces across the universe. But as soon as Indra saw Mayasura’s face flush with all the praise he had heaped on him, he changed his tone drastically.

In a split second, Indra went from smiling to glowering. His benevolent eyes had now turned menacing and hatred had replaced appreciation. Indra had gone from flattering to berating Mayasura. He began hurling verbal abuses at the much lauded builder for trying to usurp the status and position of Vishwakarma, the celestial architect. Indra’s intent was clear. He told Mayasura, “Under my regime, none can be even second to Vishwakarma. Mayasura, you have to simply cease to exist.”106 So saying, Indra invoked his infallible Vajra or thunderbolt missile. Directing the missile’s entire energy, he hurled it at the hapless Mayasura.

Hema screamed out in pain as she watched her husband writhe on the ground and succumb to the missile attack. Mayasura had left Hema a widow. Unfortunately, when she needed her sons most, neither were there by her side. That was the termination of the game of intellectual self-establishment.

matang’s curse

A sudden surge had almost touched the skies.

The unforeseen disaster was the result of neither the moon’s waxing and waning, nor the tectonic plates moving on the ocean bed. The sea creatures too did not expect this sudden rush. The ocean itself was in an intense frenzy, and foam and froth accumulated along the shore. In fact, the impact of that single colossal wave was so wide and deep that it had not only engulfed many miles of the shore, but had also hauled and flung sharks and other dangerous marine animals to the trees and left them hanging from the highest branches.

The wave was all the doing of a buffalo – a gigantic buffalo with a pair of sharp, moon-kissing horns that had come looking for a challenger. He had leaped into the ocean from a high cliff, forcing a mega tsunami to rise up and devastate the entire coast. When he saw the effect of his splash, he grunted and roared as he stirred up the foamy waters searching for a challenger.

The buffalo was one of a kind. His enormous pitch-dark body made night seem like day, and his copper-red eyes made anger look meek. His long black tail twirled restlessly, flicking the air like whips and swatting the clouds like flies. Every time he flared his nostrils, redhot fumes billowed out. Every time he ran, the earth shook under his weight. His hoof prints were the size of craters, appearing as if a series of meteors had crashed at equal distances.
