He made sure to avoid distractions and single-handedly ran the affairs of the entire kingdom without any position or title whatsoever. But now, he felt it was time to break his long silence. Time was slipping away. If he did not intervene now, the king’s life would also slip away soon.200

Hanuman walked toward the private chambers of Sugriva with the intent of offering him a lesson or two on the subtle norms of ethics. Of course, he had decided he would only say what was reasonable, appealing, beneficial, pragmatic, profitable, impressive, immaculate, imperative and impartial. As a genuine well-wisher of Sugriva, he had to shake off his sensual stupor.201

Hanuman pushed aside the guards and flung open the doors. All of a sudden, the king’s privacy was violated. Sugriva jolted up in anger at the audacity. Half influenced by liquor and half influenced by anger, he staggered up from the king-size bed and flung down his golden goblet of wine. No one had dared barge into his private chambers. But when he rubbed his drunken eyes and saw that it was Hanuman, he eased a bit. He trusted Hanuman more than himself. If Hanuman had dared to do this, there had to be a valid and important reason that would benefit only the kingdom and the king.

“It is easy to acquire a kingdom, but it is difficult to retain a friend. A stable kingdom stands on four pillars, which are most crucial for a king to keep his kingdom: ample treasury, a solid army, the king’s own sovereignty and genuine friends. A king who immerses himself in overenthusiastic self-indulgence and in the process forgets to help his friend will soon lose everything he has gained. Rama helped you when you needed Him the most. The time has come for you to express your gratitude. But you have to act on your promise before Rama reminds You of it. In the eyes of dharma, when someone does a good act after being reminded of it, the act loses its merit. Just as he had prioritized you then, you should prioritize Him now. Be a true friend and render timely help.202

Rama does not really need our help. He can turn the earth upside down in search of His wife. He is capable of tearing open the skies. Let alone rakshasas, even all the gods put together cannot resist His prowess. By helping him, we will not be doing Him any favor but would instead be offering Him our gratitude for what He has done for us.

Let us venture out right away. Order the search for Sita without further ado. You have ten million monkeys under your command. Order us, and we will not leave one stone unturned in the mission of locating mother Sita.”

Inspired by his most trusted advisor’s words, Sugriva struck the gong summoning the commander of the vanarasena, Nila. “Fifteen days,” he instructed Nila, “I give you fifteen days to gather the entire monkey army together. Ensure that all chief officers organize their troops together and march toward Kishkinda. If any monkey, no matter how heroic, arrives a day later than the stipulated period, he should be killed with immediate effect.”

As Nila and Hanuman made their way out, determined to carry out the royal command, the still drunk Sugriva chuckled. He staggered right back into his chamber, closed the door and resumed his revelry: the music, hips and wine flowed. The warning notwithstanding, nothing really changed inside.203

“As Sita is lost to Ravana and Sugriva is lost to lust, time is lost to us. Hope seems lost, too. The four rainy months are long over, but there is no sign of Sugriva. The charming sharat ritu (autumn season) that generally pleases a parched heart is here, but it is causing a burning pain to My lonely heart. The rainy season has cleaned away the dust and dirt from the whole environment, and everything is spotlessly clean like a saint’s mind. But why hasn’t Sugriva been cleansed of his bad qualities?” Rama lamented as soon as he saw the season change.

“When the rainy season ends, kings venture out for invasion. But I see no sign of Sugriva striving or even attempting to step out of his bedroom, away from the arms of the charming vanara women. These four months of separation from Sita have been like a hundred years for Me, but I am sure they have passed as quickly as a single day for Sugriva. Time flies away when immersed in sensual pleasures and limps painfully for one in separation from his beloved. Unmindful of his promise to Me, Sugriva is wallowing in the mire of carnal pleasures. O brother, it is time to shake him up and remind him of his responsibilities.204

“As long as I have you by my side, I do not need another accomplice. But I cannot tolerate those leaders who do not honor their commitments. Give Sugriva a hint of your fury and warn him that I haven’t sealed the path through which I sent Vali. I still have the arrow with which I slayed Vali. Anyone who transgresses the path of truth will be sent through the same pathway. Look at that male sparrow in that nest spending time with his beloved. On the other hand, I am wounded by the arrows of the god of love, and I am miserably lonely. King Janaka had entrusted Me with his delicate daughter Sita in the presence of the fire altar. It’s time for Sugriva to come now and help Me find My goddess.” Almost as an afterthought, Rama added, “Tell him that if he does not come, then there will be no trace of monkeys left in the universe. I will personally destroy all of them.”

By then, Lakshmana was furious, too. Gripping his bow and holding it high above his head, Lakshmana declared, “I will kill that ungrateful monkey Sugriva right now. I will then crown Angad as the king of monkeys and immediately order the start of the search for Sita. There will be no more time lapse now.”

When Rama saw Lakshmana charge toward Kishkinda with his resolve, He intervened. He had surely expressed His displeasure toward Sugriva’s inaction, He had even mentioned that He would annihilate him if need be, but He hadn’t meant that it had to be done now. It was to be the last resort, only if Sugriva remained noncooperative. He realized that Lakshmana only understood the language of extremes and that He had to be careful while giving instructions to His easily excited younger brother. Rama now worded His instructions more carefully.205

“O Lakshmana, I want you to give up your anger and have a dialogue with Sugriva in a friendly manner. Fear acts in two ways: it either makes you run away from God or run toward God. When too much fear is induced, people tend to run away from God, but when fear is induced in the right measure, it inspires people to run toward God. Lakshmana, I want you to make Sugriva fearful. But don’t frighten him so much that he runs away from us. Make sure that his affinity toward us stays intact, yet at the same ensure that you shake off his stupor.”206

lakshmana’s offence

A huge vanara crashed into the outer gates of Kishkinda, stunning everyone at the outpost. One look at the soldier, and they realized that he had been posted many hundreds of meters away to warn of danger. It looked like the intruder hadn’t even given him the chance to blow the warning bugle. In the next second, his partner also fell with the same force. The gruesome deaths of two soldiers forced the guards to order the shutdown of the city gates. Guards posted on the high walls had to raise an alarm as soon as the intruder entered within the visible range. Secured with double-sealed clamps, rocks and other heavy objects, the gates loomed as the first line of protection the city needed now.

No sooner were the gates shut, than the guards on the walls blew their warning bugles. The enemy was near. The gate chief climbed up the wall to see for himself the audacious intruder. It can’t be! The chief rubbed his eyes. It was unbelievable, but he saw Lakshmana, the brother of Rama, charging toward the city in absolute rage. His taut muscles, knitted brows, clenched fist, tightly held bow and aggressive walk were proof that Lakshmana was here to annihilate the entire city of monkeys.207

The chief shed his panic and mustered the courage to bark out orders to a division to take position. He knew that the gates wouldn’t withstand the onslaught for long. The monkeys picked up their maces, rocks, trees and clubs and braced themselves.

Lakshmana reached the sealed gates in a snap. He began laughing hysterically as he scanned the gates. The shocked monkeys were clueless about what had triggered his laughter. Before they could figure things out, they felt a big jolt. The sound that followed was deafening. The disoriented soldiers watched the city gate explode into smithereens. Clamps, boulders and harnesses shot off in different directions, killing and injuring any soldier unfortunate enough to be in the trajectory. With just one crashing entrance, dozens of monkey soldiers posted on the wall perished.

Lakshmana’s face was flush with fury and his lips were still quivering when he planted his feet on the ground. A single kick! The monkeys were amazed by the destruction a single kick could cause. The formidable, firmly fastened gates and the towering walls weren’t strong enough to withstand Lakshmana’s impactful thrust. The cracks that ran through the fractured walls enclosing Kishkinda were no different from blows to the shattered confidence of the monkeys. When they saw many of their troop mates give up hope and become listless, some abandoned their posts to inform their superiors, while some just scampered away into the protective confines of the forest.208

Word of the commotion at the gates reached Prince Angad, who rushed to the scene. One look at the livid eyes of the kind Lakshmana was enough for the intelligent Angad to decode the root of his fury. Angad had seen the unseen: Lakshmana as the five-faced hissing serpent. Red blood shot eyes glared at the young prince. Angad thought it wiser to keep quiet in all humility and hear what Lakshmana had to say. His submissiveness softened the angry messenger. He said, “O boy, tell the king of my arrival. Tell him I am waiting at his doorstep. I have come to offer him advice. It would be in his best interest to hear me out. And remember, if he doesn’t come out, I will come in!”209

Angad trotted back into the palace. Anger was no stranger to the boy. His father was anger personified. But this was another level of anger. It had a magnitude he had never seen or known before. He didn’t even how to describe it or name it. No words seemed apt enough to describe the seething emotion Lakshmana exhibited. Running and stumbling, Angad reached the inner chambers of Sugriva.

Sugriva, as was his wont these days, was oblivious to the happenings outside. Beautiful women sat around him, massaging his body as he rested his liquor-stoned head on Ruma’s lap. “He has come! He has come!” Angad screamed.

Sugriva woke up with a jolt. As he pried open his revelry-weary eyes to shout at the noisemaker, Angad gripped his feet tightly. “Soumitri has come!”

So? Sugriva could not fathom the urgency in the news of Lakshmana’s arrival. He wondered what was so special about it. He had been to the palace before and would be at the palace again, so how was this visit any different? Just because a guest was home, the king’s slumber didn’t have to be disrupted so abruptly. Sugriva’s tolerance levels were pushed again. Hanuman had barged in not so long ago just like this, and now Angad. Things have to be set straight this time. People must understand that I am king and I am in charge. No one messes with me. Sugriva’s thoughts fueled his annoyance.

He shifted to kick Angad out of the way but stopped when he heard loud screeching and screaming. Then suddenly, a tumultuous roar lifted from below – the sound of a mob in fear. Just as Sugriva tried to understand what was happening, two of his most trusted ministers, Plaksha and Prabhava, rushed into his chamber and flanked Angad. The monkeys’ collective uproar and the urgency on the face of the monkeys in his room forced Sugriva to sacrifice the comforts of his cozy bed and stand up. His coppery red eyeballs swept back and forth, as he tried to make sense of the happenings. Sugriva’s flower garland and body ornaments were tangled. He wobbled to the balcony and tried to focus his vision on the confusion outside. As opposed to the calm coziness inside, he saw disturbing disquiet outside: bewildered monkeys scurrying across, crying female monkeys, wailing infants, cracked city walls and demolished city gates. Sugriva’s eyes stopped at the bow-wielding figure standing at the pounded entrance. Lakshmana! A troop of monkey soldiers stood trembling a little distance away from the fuming prince.

What could have led to Lakshmana’s sudden demonstration of fury? Sugriva scratched his head, trying to recollect anything he may have said or done to infuriate Rama’s brother. Sugriva wondered aloud, “In all honesty, I don’t remember a single word I spoke against the brothers or a single misdeed I committed against them. What could have caused such a violent transformation in Lakshmana? Surely, he wouldn’t do such a severe thing without prior sanction from his brother Rama. It is quite possible some rival of mine has filled Rama’s ears with fabricated stories about my shortcomings. It is very easy to gain friends but so difficult to retain them. Even a single rumor can cause a rift. Let us first ascertain the cause of Lakshmana’s rage. Angad, with due respect, invite and escort Lakshmana into the palace. We will surely relieve him of his anxiety and comfort him by adopting the right measures.”210

tara’s logic

As Angad and Sugriva’s ministers ushered Lakshmana into the city, confused vanaras of different shapes and sizes stood on either side of the procession with folded palms. Taking in every small detail of the city, Lakshmana’s watchful eyes surveyed everything minutely. Kishkinda’s city design was unique. Fine architectural wonders combined with natural wonders intricately and seamlessly. It had mansions built alongside waterfalls in the mountains that cascaded down to form a pure water lake in the courtyards. Colorful villas, multistoried buildings, flower gardens, fruit orchards, and ornate archways dotted the city. The city was full of fashionably dressed vanaras in gorgeous garments, garlands and ornaments. Lakshmana noticed some shapeshifters, too. The sights and smells of the city intrigued him. The fragrances were a heady mix of sandalwood, aloe, lotus and different types of wine. As Lakshmana walked deeper into the city, the wine smells became overwhelming. Most people in most corners of the city seemed engaged in brewing wines. It was evident there was much demand and more people to drink. By the time Lakshmana was near the palace, the surroundings reeked of liquor.211

The two rows of huge palaces of the vanara chiefs flanked the central street, which ended at the most spectacular and lavish palace in all of Kishkinda. The palace of Sugriva, the king of monkeys, was the grandest palace of all, almost like that of the king of the heavens, Indra. Sunlight reflecting off the sparkling white marble gave it a magnificent luster. Gigantic and heavily armed vanaras at the palace gates looked mean and tough. The palace seemed impenetrable. After passing through seven courtyards, Lakshmana was in the meeting chamber of Sugriva’s palace. Golden and silver seats with priceless upholstery at several places in meeting hall had a certain kind of seriousness that was overridden by the flippant romantic tune played by a band of musicians. Everything about the palace reflected the king’s amorous mood: scantily clad women with tinkling silver anklets and golden waist-belts roamed the hallways and sashayed in and out of different rooms inciting lust.

TWWWWWANGGGGGGGG!

Suddenly, the entire palace trembled as if hit by an earthquake. Everyone froze and everything went topsy-turvy. Metallic objects clanged to the floor, chandeliers crashed to the ground, wine glasses slipped away from many hands and splashed on the walls, creating stains. What happened? was the question running through everyone’s panic-stricken mind. The palace was in utter chaos. A single entity was unfazed: Lakshmana.

The vanaras were aghast when they discovered that the twanging of Lakshmana’s bowstring caused the tremor. That the ungrateful vanaras were immersed in revelries while his brother Rama was languishing in sorrow infuriated Lakshmana to the core. He vented his anger by twanging his bow to serve as a resounding reminder to the ungrateful ally.212

In a matter of seconds, Lakshmana’s bowstring-twang succeeded where Hanuman’s warnings and Angad’s requests could not. It seemed people only understood the language of fear. In his cocoon of safety, Sugriva, jolted by the reverberation, trembled from head to toe. He felt as if the epicenter of this mighty quake was right under his feet. Every upholstery and decorative item in his room had crashed. All the effect of the wine he had been drinking for days together vanished in that one intense moment. He could now see clearly that his future would be bleak unless he used his intelligence and did something quick.213

She is brilliant in the analysis of critical situations and in suggesting effective solutions. Sugriva remembered his brother’s departing words. “Look up to her for guidance for your own good. She is a wise and far sighted counselor,” Vali had said. Perhaps that was a forewarning about just this mishap. What could be a more critical situation than this?

Sugriva immediately turned to Tara and consulted her. “I had always thought Lakshmana was a very mellow person. What do you think caused this change in his personality? What could I have done to trigger this violent act? You are very adept at analyzing life situations. Help me understand Lakshmana’s thought process. Better still, I think it would be best if you dealt with him yourself and pacified him. Tara, you are an eloquent speaker, soft-spoken and wise with your words. Even one with a heart as hard as a diamond will soften on hearing your words. Pacify him with your skill of speech. He will not be able to stay displeased in your presence. Great men never behave atrociously with women. At the appropriate time and once he relaxes after listening to you, I shall come and serve him.”

On realizing that her husband needed her intervention, Tara stood up from the bed, but faltered. She wasn’t sober either. Her head began to reel because she was just as intoxicated. She staggered to her feet and grabbed Sugriva’s shoulder for balance. She stabilized herself and smiled. That smile spoke a million words to Sugriva. He immediately felt relieved. The intoxication notwithstanding, Tara seemed to be in control of her mind and knew exactly what would work on Lakshmana. She had had a chance to study him on two occasions in the past and that gave her enough understanding of his psychology.214

Numerous seductive women flanked Tara as she walked down halfconscious of her surroundings. She was the queen of Kishkinda, but she hadn’t even bothered to spruce up before venturing out to meet Lakshmana. First, this was an emergency; it needed her immediate attention. Second, she wanted to appear before Lakshmana in her current state to make a pertinent point in her attempt to pacify him. Having just come off a lovemaking session, Tara looked sloppy. Exhaustion showed on her otherwise extremely pretty face, and her bloodshot eyes appeared to droop from her wine binge. Her disheveled clothes kept slipping off, and her brilliant gold waistband had unclasped and was dangling carelessly. Her white armlets on either hand were the only ornaments that stayed in place. Soon Tara and her army of women assistants encircled Lakshmana. This sudden onslaught of females made Lakshmana uncomfortable, and he immediately hung his head down to avoid making any eye contact with them. Seeing Lakshmana’s discomfort, Tara smiled knowingly. This was the exact effect she had wanted and anticipated to pacify his anger. Surprise is the best way to deal with angry people.215

Her introductory words dripping with sarcasm stumped Lakshmana. “O prince, I had heard a secret about you, but now I see that was just a rumor. I had heard that you could never stay away from your brother for more than a few minutes. Now that you have been here for a while now, how are you still alive? Surely your errand must be so urgent and important to have kept you away from Him for so long.”

Lakshmana’s reaction suggested to Tara that her words had had the desired effect. She giggled a little to irritate him further. Lakshmana was beyond surprised – he was flabbergasted. How dare anyone speak to me in this tone? Which woman could read my mind so accurately and challenge me this way? Lakshmana’s mind was filled with too many questions. He had to see the person that spoke with such eloquence and candor. He looked up: another surprise! He immediately recognized Queen Tara. But she seemed so messy for a public appearance. He couldn’t fathom why a refined queen such as Tara would step out so inappropriately dressed. But what surprised him most was her posture. She was standing in a bent posture, indicating her humility and respect for Lakshmana. Although she had questioned Lakshmana in the beginning, she didn’t really expect an answer. That question was to just shake him up and catch him on the wrong foot. More than anything else, she wanted him to think of something else that could help her deal with his anger better.

She then came to the point. “O prince, may I know the cause of your violent anger? Who would dare to play with fire and think that he wouldn’t be burned? Anyone who dares to offend you is the greatest fool.”

Lakshmana realized that it was best to talk to Tara. At least she was reasonable and intelligent in her dealings. “O queen, your husband Sugriva is the greatest fool. He is completely engrossed in sensual pleasures and neglecting his duties toward his kingdom and agonized friends. He is drinking away his fortune and deliberately sinking his future into the bottomless pit of self-indulgence. Sugriva has been ungrateful toward the very friend who has given him all that he is relishing right now. He took a favor from a friend and totally forgot about it. The stipulated four months of the rainy season are over now. Yet your churlish husband has taken no action to show his inclination to help Rama.”216

Tara was prepared to answer this. She had anticipated this allegation toward Sugriva and had expected this to be the reason behind his anger. She smiled and said, “You are blaming Sugriva for being prone to lust. The way I see it, being prone to anger is just as bad as indulging in lust. When an exalted person like you has a fault, how can you expect an ordinary monkey to be flawless?”217

Tara’s argument befuddled Lakshmana, and he fumbled to find answers. Sensing his embarrassment, Tara continued to speak. “I can understand the frustration that Lord Rama is experiencing and the helplessness He feels because of His separation from Mother Sita. I am aware of His anger toward Sugriva for his negligence. I admit to the time lapse on our part to execute the task as we had promised. But I also know of the power of desires. I am not sure if you know that time moves in accordance to the emotional state of a human. When immersed in sensual pleasures, time moves rapidly, but when drowning in sorrow, time is excruciatingly slow. The four months that have moved in the blink of an eye in the Kishkinda city has moved exceedingly slowly in that cave on the Malyavan hill. Please understand that Sugriva has been deprived of pleasures and bodily comforts for so long that no amount of indulgence seems enough for him. A person captivated by the influence of lust loses track of time. Aren’t you familiar with the story of your guru Vishwamitra who was totally immersed in a torrid affair with apsara Meneka for a good ten years before even realizing how much time he had spent away from his spiritual pursuits? If such an exalted and highly selfcontrolled person like Vishwamitra can become carried away for so long, then an ordinary monkey like Sugriva forgetting his duties and being absorbed in wanton lust for four months shouldn’t be surprising at all.218

“Respected Lakshmana, you are superior to us in every way. Superior personalities should be magnanimous and forgive the shortcomings of their inferiors. As for your allegation that Sugriva has neglected his duties and is ungrateful, I beg to differ. He is well aware of his duties and is very mindful of his promise to Rama. Despite his overindulgence, he has also managed to carry out his duties. He has dispatched his best men to summon the massive vanarasena from across the world. They will be soon here in hundreds and thousands and millions.”219

Lakshmana couldn’t admire the intelligence of this vanara woman enough. She not only cleverly managed to subdue his anger but also logically allayed all his doubts. Tara then hospitably ushered him to the inner chambers of the palace.

As soon as Lakshmana entered Sugriva’s chamber, what he saw caused a volcanic eruption of his dormant anger. Unmindful of his surroundings, Sugriva was in a tight embrace with Ruma. Dressed in silken clothes and glittering jewelry, wearing a fragrant garland, the drunken Sugriva was singing away, swirling his golden goblet of wine. Sugriva seemed unfazed by the occurrence of serious events in the palace. He wasn’t even conscious enough of Lakshmana’s entry into his chamber. Even Tara was so embarrassed by Sugriva’s behavior that she chose not to say anything for the time being. She wanted him to face Lakshmana’s wrath – something she had tried to shield him against. How could he be so shameless amid such crisis?220

Lakshmana grabbed a flower vase from near him and flung it on the floor to shake him of his stupor. He glared at Sugriva with his bloodshot eyes. Breathing heavily, Lakshmana flared up like a firestorm. Sugriva hurriedly hopped off his golden seat, dislodging Ruma and the other women from the couch. He shook himself back to reality, but wavered like a flag in the wind. When he managed to find his balance, he hung his head in shame and humility. Lakshmana reprimanded Sugriva with the harshest of words, rattling the perverse monkey king.

“Atonement for a drunk, a thief, one who has veered from austerity and even for the killer of a cow can all be found in the Scriptures, but no atonement is prescribed for one who is ungrateful. The codes of law deem that the one who breaks his promise of giving a horse is a sinner guilty of killing a hundred horses and that the one who goes back on his promises to give a cow is a sinner guilty of killing a thousand cows. But for one who breaks the promise given to a friend is deemed a sinner guilty of suicide and killing his ancestors. A treacherous person who achieves his means through his friends and does not reciprocate when the friend is in dire need is definitely the most uncultured and ungrateful person in the world. You may have become a king, but if you do not keep your promise, know that the path that took Vali’s life is not closed yet. Rama still has the arrow with which he eliminated Vali.”221

Lakshmana’s threat left Sugriva speechless. He didn’t know what to or what not to say. He had so many things in his mind to counter Lakshmana’s allegations, but words failed him. Seeing that the situation was getting out of control, Tara intervened yet again. This time, instead of speaking, she pushed open the door to the balcony of Sugriva’s inner chamber and directed Lakshmana to look out of the balcony.

A curious Lakshmana looked down and beamed, impressed with what he saw. Noticing that Lakshmana was clearly impressed, Tara said pointing to what was in front of her, “As much as Rama depends on Sugriva to help him find Sita, Sugriva depends on this monkey army. The demon who kidnapped Sita is not a loner. He has the backing of millions, maybe billions or probably trillions, of rakshasa units. Besides, no one knowns for sure where Ravana has kept Sita. Since flying off with Her forcefully in his flying machine, he could have kept Her captive anywhere in the world. So a search would entail a thorough combing operation across the globe. Regaining Sita from Ravana would involve fighting against a sea of demons. While Rama is only thinking of finding Sita at this point, Sugriva has taken things one step ahead and begun implementing the action plan to bring Her back.”222

Tara continued with her reassurances, as Lakshmana seemed attentive to what she was saying. “Sugriva hasn’t forgotten Rama’s favor. He has started the groundwork for the major operation that is about to begin soon. Of course, Sugriva has made two mistakes in this process. First, he didn’t follow protocol and inform Rama that he had begun the task of gathering the monkey army as soon as the monsoon season ended. Second, he used that waiting period as another opportunity for self-indulgence. O Lakshmana, Rama would be proud of Sugriva’s preparation, although he may not be happy with his sloppy communication style. If everything goes as per Sugriva’s calculations, the entire formidable monkey force should be arriving today.”223

By now, Lakshmana was pacified and pleased. The sight below and Tara’s words filled him with hope. Sugriva saw the change in Lakshmana’s mood and realized this was his only chance to seek redemption. Just as a damp cloth when wrung releases water, Lakshmana’s smile released Sugriva fear. He spoke with deep emotion while expressing his point of view. “Everything I have today is only because of the benevolence of the worship-worthy Rama. No one can dare to repudiate a word given to Rama unless it is a suicidal attempt. Rama does not really need my help. Someone who has the power to uproot seven gigantic trees, an entire mountain and earth itself with a single arrow, does not need any assistance to bring back His wife. I know for sure that in this venture, Rama will do the needful and we will just be bystanders admiring His prowess. For the honor of serving Rama in any way, I am ready to abdicate as king and renounce my riches, my food grains, my livestock and my own wife. Please pardon me, if I have overstepped my role as a servant. My main mistake was lack of communication. I remained disconnected from you for the past four months completely. But probably, the greater mistake is my habit of drinking. This habit has destroyed my ability to discriminate. I was so absorbed in drinking that I forgot my responsibilities toward my kingdom and its citizens.”

Sugriva ripped apart the garland on his neck and threw it on the ground. He then made a vow with Lakshmana as witness. “I discard this drinking habit of mine from today onward. If I touch liquor, I will become a sinner for Rama.”

Sugriva fell on his knees in front of Lakshmana and appealed to his kindness. “My dear Lakshmana, forgive me for my lapses. Who doesn’t make mistakes? If you go on punishing your own people for every small mistake they make, then very soon you will be left with no one.”224

Lakshmana picked up Sugriva and embraced him. He encouraged him and appreciated his genuineness. Indeed, Rama’s choice was perfect. They did indeed have a sixth brother225 now!

sins absolved

Sugriva and Lakshmana stepped out of the royal palanquin and entered the spacious cave where Rama sat in deep thought about Sita. As soon as he stepped into the cave, Sugriva experienced purity and subtle vibrations. Definitely, Rama has been practicing some intense tapasya to be able to transform the ambience of an ordinary cave into that of a holy place.

Sugriva fell at Rama’s feet and begged forgiveness for the delay he had caused in beginning His mission. Rama picked him up lovingly and embraced him. As Sugriva rested his head on Rama’s chest, he felt that this was the highest reward he could get in his life. He then signaled his guards to bring in the gifts. One by one, the vanaras trooped in with trays of fruits, roots and medicinal herbs and placed them all in front of Rama.

Sugriva held Rama’s hand gently and brought Him out of the cave to show Him a spectacle he wanted him to see. Walking him to the edge of the cliff, Sugriva said majestically, “Lo and behold, my master!”

Rama and Lakshmana together glanced below the cliff. Unbelievable! Incredible! Inconceivable!

Millions of vanara soldiers stood in rows. Each vanara stood with folded palms. Seeing Rama and Lakshmana gaze at them from high above, every one of them raised their folded hands over their heads. Their closed palms resembled lotus buds and their bodies, lotus stems.

As Rama and Lakshmana looked with great surprise at the massive army gathered below, Sugriva felt content. The mission had just begun.

## Chapter 7

A Storm Gathers

Battle-Ready Monkeys

The vanarasena spread as far as the eye could see: every single inch of landmass, mountain peaks, water bodies and even trees were filled with them. Sugriva stood surveying his army with his hands on his hips. He took note of the chiefs who were present, and the strength of the units rallied behind each of them, standing according to their ranks. Rama and Lakshmana flanked him, admiring the extent of the armed forces of the vanara king. This was definitely impressive!

Eager to introduce Rama to all his leaders, Sugriva began directing His attention toward each of the prominent leaders in the vanarasena. He said, “Our army has sixty-seven chiefs who manage this entire colossal monkey army.” He walked Rama to a corner of the cliff from where he wanted to begin the introduction. Sugriva pointed out and said, “You have already met some of the leaders and some you are yet to meet. The one holding his arms high, knitting his eyebrows in fury, resembles a tall mountain peak, has the complexion of a lotus petal and whose angry tail-lashing can be heard in ten directions is Angad. He is the mighty son of Vali and the prince of Kishkinda. When he walks, the earth feels burdened under the power of his strides.

“The one standing close by with a golden sheen and seems as lofty as a mountain is Sushena. He is the son of the monkey Dharma, is an incarnation of Varuna, the ocean god, and the father of Tara. He is an expert in the science of medicine and in the art of warfare, and he leads an army of ten billion monkeys.

“Close by is Sharabha, the son of Parjanya, the god of rain. He dwells on the Salveya mountains and is the commander of the monkeys. Look at him yawning continuously and shaking his ears. He has never retreated from a battle, instead he charges and moves ahead demonstrating his rage. He has absolutely no fear of death. Although his eyes are askew, his vigor is sharp. When he lashes out with his tail and roars like a lion, his enemies tremble in fright. He commands viharas, the hundred and forty thousand army chiefs who wander fearlessly.

“You have already met our dear hero, Hanuman. Now I would like to introduce you to his father, the heroic army chief Kesari. He wanders about in the Kanchana mountains famous for its all-season fruit-bearing trees. The plateau of that mountain is the abode of great sages. He leads thousands of monkeys that are both tawny and white. They believe in the sheer power of their fists and nails. Some of them have four tusks, and they are more formidable than the most ferocious of lions. They have long coiling tails that can slash the guts out of their enemies.

“Look down at that monkey with the long mane. He is Rambha, and he resides in the mountains of Krishna and Sahya on the Vindhya ranges. He has thirty billion super excellent monkeys under his wing. They will follow him blindly until the end of the earth if need be.

“Next to him is the silver-hued monkey, Sweta. He is extremely quick-witted and is known for his terrific prowess. He is a warrior par excellence in the three worlds. Beside him is another armychief named as Kumuda. His long coppery-yellow hair gives him a distinctive look. He is the leader of a hundred thousand monkeys and can spread terror in war. To Kumuda’s right is Hara. The hair color on his tail is very interesting and unique. The tail is long and soft, with patches of red, yellow, white and ‘super-white’; it shines as he drags it along the ground at times or keeps it erect like a stick. Hundreds and thousands of monkeys rally behind him with a great desire to please him and win his favor.

“Close by is the general Panasa who is like a cloud, and his voice – which resembles the roll of drums, is the voice of the monkey army. The extremely tall chief is from the Pariyatra mountains and is invincible in any battle. Next is the fierce and boastful Kanda, who yearns to fight and resides on the Ramya mountains on the banks of the Gomati river. Then there is this army lieutenant Krathana who has the hue of red chalk and keeps many units of courageous and strong monkeys under his wing. Right next to him is Dambha. Whatever he does is stunning. Whether in fury, or leaping, or standing still, everyone is helplessly attracted to him and keeps staring at him. He resides in the Sahasraksha mountains with his bulky troop.

“The one with a frightful appearance, great strength and accompanied by ten billion baboons with black faces is the troop leader Gavaksha. Stories of his adventures and sounds of his roars reach the enemies much before he reaches them, and together they shatter their confidence to smithereens. Immediately adjacent to him is commander Vinata, who resembles the Dardura mountain in size. His six-million strong monkey army resides on the seashore, and when they are together, they resemble another ocean. He is the one who leads the army from the front, inspiring everyone with his charm. Also present here is the valorous Shatabali. He is followed by a hundred billion vanaras. To his right are the gorilla twins Mainda and Dvividha, the ashwini kumaras. Each commandeers hundreds of thousands of vanaras.

“You have already met my closest companions, Hanuman, the son of Vayu, the wind god; Taru, the son of Brihaspati, the guru of the demigods; Nila, the son of Agni, the god of fire and Nala, the son of Vishwakarma the celestial architect. Thousands and thousands of vanara troops accompany these heroes.

“Nila, you should know, is an engineer, a chemist and a great scientist. He knows the scientific properties of every substance in creation. He can manipulate matter so expertly that it could change color, smell and properties of the substances totally. Not only does he know to deal with substances, he is also an expert in dealing with the mind. He could alter the state of the mind using chemically induced substances. His drugs can make a miserable person feel happy or make a happy person angry. But by far, his greatest talent is his architectural ability. The construction of the entire kingdom of Kishkinda has happened under his supervision. His specific expertise is creating massive interlocking stone structures that can stand the test of time for centuries.226

“Let me familiarize you with four special vanaras. Here are Sharabha, Kumuda, Vahni and Rambha. These four lead teams of vanaras who are guise-changing experts, who can change their forms at will and within seconds. Their count is unknown as it’s the secret troop of monkeys.

“Now for the most interesting and the most experienced part of the vanarasena. Here is Dhumra, the master of two billion bears that move at the speed of lightning. He is probably the oldest of the vanarasena. Next to him is his brother who is universally famous for his herculean tasks. He is Jambavan and appears like a black cloud. His has a hundred-million strong bear brigade. He is mightier than his brother in all ways and definitely the most intelligent person on this planet right now. His age, knowledge, experience and practical wisdom make him the most trusted and valued person in the vanara clan. O Rama, what I have introduced to you is only a fraction of the entire monkey army. But this should give you an idea of the structure and might of the vanarasena.”227

Sugriva’s systematic introduction of the vanara chiefs was fascinating. Rama and Lakshmana were impressed by the show of strength lined up in front of them. For the first time in months, Rama smiled jubilantly. Hope surged. When He looked at Sugriva this time, there was pride in His eyes. Sugriva had definitely showed Him a higher prospect. With this kind of an army in search for Sita, it was impossible not to find Her.228

With one signal from Sugriva, all sixty-seven vanara chiefs made their way to the top of the cliff and stood in a line. They had all carried a small offering for Rama. Each came forward, handed over his token of appreciation to Rama and stepped back to his place. Soon Rama and Lakshmana were flooded with flowers, fruits, roots and herbs of various kinds. The brothers were told that these edible fruits and roots were extremely special and had been obtained from the rarest and most inaccessible places on earth. If anyone consumed even one of these divine fruits, the person would remain satiated for at least a month. The flowers and herbs had medicinal properties that could cure any disease or ailment. Lakshmana and Hanuman together carried the offerings to put them inside Rama’s cave for future use. Expressing his gratitude to all the monkey chiefs for having come from such far off places with their entire armies at such a short notice, Rama folded his hands and smiled at them. His humility and gentle demeanor melted their hearts. Each decided at that very instant that they would scour the world and even lay down their lives for this person. Suddenly they all seemed to have found a purpose in life.229

Walking toward the edge of the cliff and scanning the huge vanarasena that stood ahead, Rama asked Sugriva for the numerical calculations of the army size. Sugriva smiled and went on to cite some overwhelming figures. “O Lord, there is a very interesting method of calculations that we follow in keeping count of the armies. Ten thousand into thousand is ten million. A trillion is a shanku. A hundred thousand shanku make a maha-shanku. A hundred thousand maha-shanku make a vrinda. A hundred thousand vrinda make a maha-vrinda. A hundred thousand maha vrindas make a padma. A hundred thousand padmas make a maha-padma. A hundred thousand maha-padmas make a kharva. A hundred thousand kharvas make a maha-kharva. A hundred thousand maha-kharvas make a samudra. A hundred thousand samudras make an ogha. A hundred thousand oghas make a mahaugha.

“O Lord Rama, Your army now consists of a total of a trillion shanku, a thousand maha-shanku, a hundred vrinda, a thousand maha-vrinda, a hundred padma, a thousand maha-padma, a hundred kharva, a hundred samudra and a hundred mahaugha. The vastness of this army competes with the vastness of the oceans.”230 231

The brothers were startled by the unbelievable figures Sugriva just threw at them. How were they to rally such a huge army? That didn’t really matter, in any case. It seemed that Sugriva and his chiefs had everything under control down to the minutest details. From what they saw, success would be the only outcome in this venture. Optimism was high, motivation remarkable and enthusiasm inexplicable. Now the quest for Sita had to begin!232

Sensing Rama’s impatience, Sugriva asked Him to command the army now. “O Lord, the vanarasena is highly accomplished in all ways. In vigor and valor, they match and even exceed the might of the rakshasas. They can traverse the earth with no limitations. They can tread the most inaccessible and hazardous landmasses. They can swim in the deepest of water bodies. They can fly in the skies. They have the power to lift mountains. They have the dexterity to maneuver through the most complicated jobs – physical or intellectual. Their loyalty is unquestionable, competence is unimaginable and success always guaranteed. Whatever they take up, they always accomplish, however impossible it may seem. They just don’t give up even when the goal seems unattainable. This highly disciplined army abides by my orders. I hand over the charge of this entire army to You. My entire army and I are totally at Your disposal now. Command us as You may please. We will carry out Your orders with total obedience. I know exactly what You want us to do, but I want my army to hear the instructions from You. I want all vanaras to identify with Your voice – the voice of their master! I want everyone to hear and follow commands issued directly by You.”233

Rama walked up to Sugriva and hugged him. Seeing their master embraced by the Lord with so much love, the vanara chiefs gasped. They hadn’t ever seen such a genuine exhibition of love. Caressing Sugriva’s head, Rama spoke, “My dear friend, I only need to know two things: Is Sita alive yet? and Where has Ravana kept Her? Only after getting this information, can we decide the further course of action. O King Sugriva, I am not competent to command this vast monkey army, nor is Lakshmana. You are most capable of performing this task. I want you to act as both my organizer as well as my instrument. We will stand by you. I request you to rally the monkeys in the best possible manner and get me these two pieces of information first. I consider you a good combination of competence and prudence. You know not only what needs to be done, but also how it needs to be done and, more importantly, when it needs to be done. O kind-hearted chief of all vanara chiefs, do go ahead and organize your army to help achieve our goals.”

As soon as Rama spoke, Sugriva’s throat choked with emotion. The confidence Rama had reposed in his abilities touched his heart. Rama’s detachment and disinterest in controlling and commanding such a huge army became a very valuable lesson in leadership to Sugriva. His appreciation and respect for Rama grew a million fold.234 Sugriva gathered his focus and looked in the direction of his chiefs. He first called aside Vinata and explained the details about his project of searching for Sita in the east. Sugriva then sent him back to his position and summoned Sushena, his father-in-law, and handed over to him the task of searching for Sita in the west. Next, he called Shatabali and painstakingly explained the scope of the search he had to undertake in the north.

wise old being

Sugriva’s treatment to the south was different. He summoned not one chief but a group of the most powerful vanara chiefs to explain the task in this direction. The list of chiefs called to head this direction was a clear indicator of the importance of the search party in this direction. He called Angad, Hanuman, Jambavan, Dvividha, Mainda, Gaja, Gavaaksha, Vrishabha, Gavaya, Sharagulma, Gandhamaadhana, Ulkaamukha and Ananga. He deputed Angad as the chief of this search party and began to address all of them. The brothers noticed the amount of attention Sugriva gave this group. Understanding the variegated dynamics of this group, Sugriva offered a very valuable input. He realized that Angad, although the prince, was still too young and inexperienced. He also realized that he ought to capitalize on the presence of the two most resourceful people in this group. Hanuman and Jambavan were in a league of their own. Hanuman with his incredible prowess and talents, Jambavan with his wisdom and experience could cater to situations where Angad’s youthful enthusiasm would fall short. Even before he went into the details of the project he was going to entrust them with, Sugriva felt that he should emphasize the role of Jambavan to the others. He wanted to make sure that the others in the team took his advice and directions seriously. He realized that Jambavan would play a crucial role in the initial mission of finding Sita and in the ensuing war to get Her back from Ravana. More than anyone else, Sugriva wanted Rama to understand the importance of this personality.235

Sugriva shared some super fascinating stories about Jambavan’s history. “Jambavan is the oldest living entity in the universe second only to Lord Brahma. Even before Lord Brahma began creating the universe and different species to inhabit it, he created Jambavan. As soon as Brahma was born on top of the lotus flower that grew from the navel of Lord Vishnu, the Lord instructed him to meditate. He was doing just that when two demons, Madhu and Kaitabha began to pollute the atmosphere with extreme negativity. All of Lord Brahma’s four faces began to perspire. Not knowing any way to handle this calamity, Brahma beseeched Lord Vishnu to save him from the ordeal. Lord Vishnu challenged Madhu and Kaitabha to a duel. Meanwhile, a drop of the sweat from the face of Lord Brahma fell on to his palm. Lord Brahma observed something strange within that drop: a very tiny entity. To satisfy his curiosity, he brought his palms very close to his eyes to make a more carful observation. Even as he was inspecting, the entity began to grow in size. Soon, it was the size of Brahma’s palm. As it began to grow in size, Brahma noticed that it was like a hairy bear. In a short span, that bear assumed a size much bigger than Lord Brahma himself.

“The bear folded his hands in obeisance and asked the creator about where to live. Brahma was astounded by how he had created such a mystical entity. He asked him to reside on the banks of Jambunada, the only existing landmass in the whole universe that wasn’t submerged in the waters of dissolution. Creation hadn’t even begun at that point. Brahma reasoned that once he created everything, he could give the bear a more suitable place to live. The bear bowed in reverence and sat down in Jambunada wondering what to do next. Just then, Lord Vishnu, who was engaged in a terrible battle with the demons, reached there. The bear found the fight very entertaining. He began clapping and hooting, cheering Lord Vishnu to defeat the demons. In fact, he was so engrossed in the fight that he even began to give ideas to the Lord on which moves to employ to disadvantage them. Lord Vishnu was extremely pleased with his sole audience. He was also extremely pleased with Madhu and Kaitabha for putting up such a tough fight. He had been yearning for a good duel. Finally, satiated, he slayed the demons in one go. The bear jumped up in joy and clapped his hands cheering the Lord. He congratulated the Lord and expressed his eagerness to witness more such fights. The Lord assured him that once creation began, he would definitely see many more thrilling battles.236

“Because he was a resident of the Jambunada island, he came to be known as Jambavan. Once the process of creation began, Jambavan became a permanent witness to every major event in the universe. During the great deluge, he was there on the boat of King Satyavrata, who saved all living entities from the waters of dissolution. To help the king, Lord Vishnu appeared as a giant golden fish with a horn. As Satyavrata harnessed the boat to the horn of the fish using the snake Vasuki as a rope, Jambavan began to circumambulate the fish. As the fish dragged the boat in through the deluge, taking it around the universe, Jambavan kept going around the fish itself. Although hundreds of yugas passed, Jambavan just wouldn’t die. In fact, with age, he got stronger and stronger. He witnessed the Kurma avatar of Lord Vishnu in action during the churning of the milk ocean. He was also there during the monumental fight between Varaha, or the boar incarnation, and the demon Hiranyaksha. He also see how Varahadev’s finally slapped Hiranyaksha to terminate him.

“He saw Narsingadev, or the half-man-half-lion incarnation, finish off Hiranyakashipu. Then when Vaamanadev, or the dwarf incarnation, manifested, Jambavan did something unbelievably interesting. To claim the three worlds, Vaamanadev extended his feet to touch the outer ceiling of the universe. When Jambavan saw the feet growing in size and height to such magnitude, he didn’t want to lose this opportunity to worship the feet of the Lord. In exactly six moments, he circled the feet of Trivikrama or Vaamana, eighteen times. He travelled a million times faster than the speed of the mind to achieve such an unbelievable task.

“Jambavan was lost in joy. While returning from the adventure, a small mishap occurred. Jambavan was so absorbed in celebrating that he didn’t notice that his toenails bruised Mount Meru. Mount Meru was offended by Jambavan’s carelessness as he was used to being worshipped and honored even by the holy sapta rishis. He cursed Jambavan with instant old age for the carelessness that stemmed from his youthfulness. That moment Jambavan became old and weak. Of course, Jambavan realized his mistake and begged forgiveness from Meru. Meru forgave him, but because he couldn’t allay the effect of his own curse, he offered him a blessing as compensation. He gave him the blessing of being a very important aide to Lord Rama’s mission when He incarnates and would serve Him using his experience and wisdom.237

“After he finished narrating Jambavan’s story, Sugriva placed the old bear’s hand into the hands of Lord Rama. Rama clasped Jambavan’s hand with both His hands with great love and affection. Sugriva continued with his introduction, “Jambavan is the oldest person alive today. There is nothing he doesn’t know. There is no god he doesn’t know. There is no incarnation he hasn’t seen. There is no ocean he hasn’t crossed. He knows every nook and cranny of this creation like the back of his hand. Angad, you must carefully and respectfully abide by his advice at all times.”

A little embarrassed by Sugriva’s eulogies in front of his eternal master Lord Rama, Jambavan said, “All that Sugriva said is history. I may know every place, but the present reality is I don’t have the ability to fly as before. I can at best be Your guide in this attempt to find Sita. With the blessings of our master, we will surely be able to find Her.”238

Rama smiled not so much on hearing Jambavan’s words, but on remembering the numerous times and situations in which He had met him in the past.

southward bound

Sugriva gathered all southbound chiefs together in a circle and addressed them seriously. He wanted all of them to hear every word he had to say.

“In my opinion, the southern direction is the most likely where we can find Mother Sita. That’s the reason I have chosen the most capable men from our vast vanarasena to scan this region. I also forewarn you that this will be, by far, the most challenging exploration you will ever venture in your life. But rest assured that with your collective abilities and cooperative endeavor, nothing can prevent you from achieving success. Do not allow negative thinking to hit you no matter how strenuous and arduous the trail gets. I will now highlight to you the path that you should take and the regions in the southern direction you should explore thoroughly.239

“The exploration of the southern direction ought to start all the way from the Vindhya mountains. I have asked all the other groups exploring the other directions to avoid the Vindhya mountains, as I want your group to explore that territory. With hundreds of beautifully illuminated peaks, the Vindhya range is possibly where Sita has been held captive. From there, continue exploring farther south, reaching the Narmada river. Narmada is the river of the gods. Every day the gods from the heavens come here to bathe in its sacred waters. The riverbed is filled with precious gems that illuminate it.

“Farther south, you will reach the Hemakuta mountain, which is where beautiful damsels from the heavens come to play. Their songs and voices are captivating. A tank close to the mountains is the habitat of creatures called asvanas, who love the music of these damsels so much that they want to listen to it long through the night. The asvana creatures kill anyone who ventures into this mountain range and disturbs this music. My sincere advice to you is to stay away from that tank. Search that area discreetly and leave immediately.

“From there, continue to explore the banks of the following rivers: River Krishnaveni, Mahanadi, Varada and Pennai. Beware of the dangerous snakes that slither in those areas. Once this is done, then venture farther south, explore the dharma countries, which are the territories of Mekhala, Utkala, the cities of Darshaarna, the kingdoms of Abravanti, Avanti, Vidarbha and Maheehaka. Finally, explore the Vanga and Kalinga territories along with that of the Kaushika territory thoroughly. Make sure when you explore these territories, you also rummage through fringe areas. That way, we leave no stone unturned.

“Next, you will explore the impassable Dandakaranya. Scan every mountain, river and cave in the vast Dandakaranya forest. This forest extends through a huge tract of land, extending all the way through the provinces of Andhra, Pundra and Chola. Within the Dandakaranya forest is the Mundaka ghata, forest filled with flowering trees. There you will find the Malaya mountain, which has many iron ore mines. This mountain is also called Agastyamalai as it is the residence of the wonder-sage Agastya. On this mountain, under the thousands of sandalwood trees, you will find innumerable sages performing their tapasya. Most of them never sleep, and many don’t even wink. This place is believed to fulfil all desires. On the topmost peak of the mountain, you will find Sage Agastya in a deep meditation. Without disturbing him, patiently wait for him to exit his meditative trance and then seek his blessings. Only when he permits, should you go farther.240

“As you descend the Malaya mountain from its western side, you will find the majestic Tamravarni river, both banks of which are lined with sandalwood trees. As the river constantly brushes against the sandalwood trees, the soft fragrant sandalwood paste mixes with the water giving it a light coppery shade; hence, the name Tamravarni, meaning copper colored. Although the river is so fragrant and attractive, beware when crossing it. There are innumerable deadly, bloodthirsty crocodiles in it.

“Farther south, you will spot the Pandu mountain, the peak of which almost touches the sky. It is so high and broad that occasionally the sun considers resting on it for some time. Ahead of the Pandu mountain, you will come across the incredible Godavari river, which is full of gold particles and precious gems. Godavari flows around the Pandu mountain graciously and resembles a wife embracing her husband.

“As you venture farther south, you will see the Shone river with banks full of flower and fruit trees. Then you will come to the majestic Suryakanta and Chandrakanta mountains. On crossing these, you will enter Konkana, the coast reclaimed by Parashuram by thrusting his axe in the ocean. Adjacent to this is Kulinda. Here you will come across the Arundhati mountain – the peaks of which touch the sky. The best of the best cannot cross that mountain, and it is completely inaccessible. But you must explore every inch of that mountain.

“After this, you will come across a very holy hill called Venkatadri. This hill is considered extremely holy and auspicious. Great ascetics and saintly people reside here permanently. They will never exchange this divine abode for any amount of riches or heavenly pleasures. For them, this place is as good as Vaikuntha, the spiritual world. Divine songs glorifying the Lord ring through the mountain. I forewarn you not to step on this holy mountain even by mistake. If you do, then this mountain with its power will erase all your sins and immediately elevate you to the spiritual world, giving you liberty. Who then will continue the exploration for finding Mother Sita? Therefore, I advise you to stay a good distance away from this holy mountain. In fact, I can say with great confidence right away that Ravana could not have kept Sita there. If he did step on this mountain, he would have gone through a change of heart and would have surely returned Sita to Rama.241

“Next, you will come across the holy Kaveri river in whose fresh waters even the apsaras from the heavens hanker to bathe. Built on the banks of this river is the powerful Pandyan kingdom that extends all the way up to the shores of the southern ocean. Explore every inch of this kingdom without fail. Once you have scoured it, you will find yourself on the southern tip of the land. On one side, you will find the massive Mahendra mountain staring at you, and on the other, you will find the vast southern ocean glaring at you. If you still don’t find any trace of Sita even here, then understand that She has been taken somewhere beyond the southern ocean.

“O vanara heroes, more than any other time during this expedition, you will need each other most at this point. I can predict your morale will be at its lowest and the vast ocean in front of you will only dent it further. This is the time to remember the importance of this mission. This is the time to be honest about your abilities and strengths. This is the time to test your physical might and mental mettle. Use the golden Mount Mahendra as your launch pad to jump across the vast ocean. This mountain is famed for its numerous varieties of flora and fauna and is frequented by celestials, even Indra, the king of the heavens.242

Your next destination is an island right in the middle of the southern ocean. This hundred-yojana wide island is a place where no human can reach. You have to explore the length and breadth of this island and not just once, but many times. I feel that Ravana has most likely held Sita captive here. This dazzling island is the abode of the demon king Ravana. Because this is a mystical place, don’t believe anyone there. Don’t even trust your own eyes. Unless you have proof beyond doubt that it is Sita, do not trust anyone even if that person seems to be like Her in every way.

Reasons for Sugriva’s Duplicity

The question that arises here is that if Sugriva already knew about Ravana’s abode then why did he send the army all over the world? More than that, why did he lie to Rama in the beginning, feigning ignorance of this fact?

There are several possible reasons for this seemingly duplicitous behavior of Sugriva.

He was being politically strategic. He feared that if he had told Rama about Ravana’s whereabouts, Rama would possibly have immediately rushed there to kill him. In that case, Sugriva’s aspirations would never have been met.

There was no real guarantee that Ravana would have actually taken Sita to Lanka after the abduction. Therefore, without being certain, had he told Rama, it would be considered mitra droha or cheating a friend who trusts you.

Sugriva himself had no idea about Ravana’s exact location but came to know about it from Tara much after Vali’s death. So in that case, when he expressed his ignorance to Rama in the beginning, it was not a blatant lie.

No thief would hide his booty in his own house. This could be Sugriva’s premise for sending the vanarasena all over the world. Also, Sugriva perhaps reasoned that if he were to send a single search party to Lanka, Ravana would come to know about the search mission and he would probably shift Sita to some unknown location. To avoid this, Sugriva chose to scan the whole world simultaneously to rule out all other possibilities.

“However, my friends, let me warn you that reaching this island is not just a matter of a leap. There is a demoness named Angarika who lives in the middle of the ocean and can be a formidable obstacle. The things she can do, you can’t even begin to imagine!243

“Assuming that you don’t find Sita after rummaging through that island exhaustively, I want you to scout some other places. On the other side of that island, farther south, is another island with a mountain named Pushpitaka. The mountain sits majestically right in the middle of the oceanic waters and its twin peaks shimmer in gold and silver. The sun adores the golden peak, while the moon loves the silver one. After you bow in reverence to that mountain, search it in meticulously. Once done, venture farther to reach another mountain named Suryavaan, which is roughly about fourteen yojanas from Pushpitaka mountain. Next, you will find the Vaidyuta mountain, which is home to the juiciest of fruit-bearing trees. I assure you that you will never find tastier honey than the one in that mountain. Beyond that is the Kunjara mountain, which is most scenic. On this mountain, Vishwakarma had built a mansion for Agastya to live with his wife Lopamudra after their marriage. Of course, Agastya left that place and began to reside in the Dandakaranya forest mountains.

“At this point in your journey, you will come across a city named as Bhogavati, which is the abode of the snakes. It is a well-built, modern city with unbelievable facilities and technology. The snakes protect the city from intrusion, no matter how small or harmless it may seem. There the king of serpents dwells along with his assistants. Search the length and breadth of Bhogavati for Sita. Don’t forget to check the fringe areas around the city premises.

“After all this, you will finally arrive at a place known as Rishabha hill. This great mountain resembles a holy bull and is a treasure trove of the most precious gems and stones you can ever find in the universe. There on that mountain, you will find sandalwood trees of different colors. Some are ocher, some lotus-leaf green, yet others are sky blue and the most attractive of them are the color of glowing fire. However intriguing the trees may seem, do not ever touch them. These sandalwood trees are guarded by a genre of gandharvas named Rohitas. They are headed by five gandharva kings Shailusha, Gramani, Shiksha, Shuka and Bhabru. These kings have resplendently effulgent physiques. This mountain marks the outer border of the extent of the southern exploration. Beyond this area, it is impossible for Ravana to take Sita, because beyond this lies the jurisdiction of Yama, the god of death. You need not even consider going beyond the Rishabha hills, because mortals have no entry beyond this point.244

“Memorize everything I have told you so far, and do not falter. Do not do a half-hearted job. Comb through each place, leave no scope for doubt before you venture further in your search. Do not mechanically abide by my instructions, use your common sense where you must. If you see places I forgot to mention, use your judgment to decide if you must explore them or not. Come what may, I want you to return within a month. Anyone who returns with the words, ‘Sita is found,’ will become my dearest friend and will enjoy luxuries beyond imagination for his entire lifetime. This is my guarantee. The harbinger of good news will be dearer to me than life itself.245

“O chiefs, I know all of you are mighty and brave. You are all born to achieve the impossible. Each of you has inherited powerful legacies. Now is the time to put everything we have to good use in the service of Lord Rama and find Mother Sita. This is the perfection of our existence.

“Go, dear chiefs. Explore. Cooperate. Succeed and gain!”

hanuman’s assignment

“Thirty days! That’s all I can wait for!” The monkey chiefs were dumbfounded by Sugriva’s emphasis on the deadline. How could he expect them to run through an entire section of the vast planet in such a short span? This is insane! Just as the monkey chiefs were about to argue the impossibility of his cut-off date, Sugriva said something that froze them. “If anyone returns without totally exploring the regions I have assigned to him or after the stipulated time, I will eliminate that impudent and disobedient rascal.”

The instructions were clear! Everyone knew that Sugriva meant every word he said and was quite capable of following through with his threats. Hadn’t he managed to eliminate the deathless Vali? Suddenly, the chiefs were all set to go about their mission, as if each could hear the rapid ticking of time. They offered their respects to their chief and Lord Rama and leaped ahead. They gathered their respective groups and began their impossible mission right away.

As millions of coordinated monkey armies began their movements in different directions, a huge dust storm arose. Rama had tears in His eyes as the monkeys surged with such enthusiasm in different directions. There was hope now. He then turned His attention back to Sugriva. Although the rest of the armies had departed to the east, west and north, Sugriva had retained the chiefs for the south. There was still something left to convey to them –something he didn’t want the rest of the army to hear, something that the groups headed in the other directions should definitely not witness.

Sugriva began to scrutinize the vanara chiefs that were to lead the expedition in the southern direction. His eyes froze on Hanuman. His heart swelled with many emotions as he looked at him. Hanuman had been such an integral part of his life over the last few years – a friend, a minister, a messenger, a protector, an advisor, a teacher, a brother and so much more. More than anything else, Hanuman had been the gift of his father Surya to him at the most crucial junction of his life. All these months and years, Hanuman had been serving Sugriva with utmost loyalty as an expression of his gratitude to his guru. Today, as Sugriva looked at this divine personality that had so far been so intricately connected with his life, he instinctively knew that Hanuman was entering another phase of his life. He always knew that Hanuman was too big for him to handle, not just physically, but mentally, intellectually and spiritually. However, thus far, Hanuman had helped him grow. Now it was time he allowed Hanuman to grow. Sugriva knew that the person who would be the catalyst for Hanuman’s growth had come. He had to allow Hanuman to grow from the small pond of Kishkinda to the ocean of Lord Rama’s service.246

With a smile on his face, Sugriva addressed Hanuman, singling him out from the group. “If Jambavan is the wisdom and Angad the youthful dynamism behind this group, then you are the strength that carries everyone along. I can very confidently state that the success of the entire mission of the search for Mother Sita rests on you. There can never be any impediment big enough to stop you in your transit on land, sea or in the sky. No animals, humans, gods, birds, underwater organisms, nocturnal demons can ever restrict your movements. With your knowledge of all the realms, your unusual mobility, your unstoppable speed, your unfathomable vivacity and unimaginable abilities, you are like your father, Vayu, the wind god. I don’t think anyone can match your prowess, astuteness, adventurous spirit or intellect in the three worlds. However, more than any of these things, your greatest asset is your spotless character. With so much and more, I have a lot of hope pinned on you. Somehow, please find Sita. You have all the resources to be able to do so.”

Rama was intrigued and inspired as He watched Sugriva single out Hanuman for such attention and praise. Hanuman had definitely impressed Him right from the moment He saw him disguised as that beggar. From that point on for a little more than the last four months, Rama had been carefully observing Hanuman. In fact, every act and speech of Hanuman had touched Rama very deeply. His sensitivity, intelligence, strength, resourcefulness, kindness, skills and servitude together made him stand out in the crowd of the vanarasena. Now that Sugriva was so effusive in his praise for Hanuman, Rama’s appreciation for Hanuman went up several notches. By this time, Rama had developed good faith in Sugriva’s abilities to judge people. Sugriva would only give such importance to someone whose record had been outstanding. In fact, Rama was now convinced that Hanuman was one of the most special beings in His world.

Tears began to roll down Hanuman’s eyes as soon as Rama uttered His first words to him directly in the last four months. “O Hanuman, I totally depend on you now!”247

Hanuman’s heart melted with Rama’s first words of dependence. Rama continued, “I am convinced you will succeed in the current expedition. With this conviction, what I am going to offer you right now is the most valuable thing in the universe!”

Rama held out something glittering in His right hand. Hanuman couldn’t really see what He was holding very clearly. Yet, he bent down on one knee, cupped his palms and extended them to Rama. Rama dropped that glittering object into his palms. As soon as it touched Hanuman’s hands, a shiver ran through his body. On taking a look, Hanuman realized that it was a golden ring studded with a large precious stone on top and two small stones below. It definitely looked invaluable. Hanuman noticed inscribed on the larger top stone the syllable Shri was and on the lower stones, the syllables Ra and ma.

The Ring

Originally, this ring was Sita’s. It couldn’t have been Rama’s. A humble person like Rama wouldn’t arrogantly wear a ring with His name inscribed on it. Sita wore this ring on Her finger to remind Her always of the protection of Rama’s name in Her life. When the boatman Kevat ferried them across the Ganga, Sita had given this ring to Rama to offer it as a token of gratitude to Kevat. He hadn’t of course accepted it. From then on, Rama had retained the ring on his finger. Sita had wanted to ask Rama back then about why He had kept the ring, but She let it go, concluding that Her husband always backed His action with some good reason. Rama had probably known that this ring would unite them someday.

“As soon as She sees the ring, She will know that you are My envoy. When you see Her, remind Her about the first time My eyes met Hers in Mithila, when I had come to attend the bow sacrifice. Everyone thinks that We first met after I broke Lord Shiva’s bow, but the reality is that We had seen each other the previous day. She was standing in Her palace, and I was walking by with my guru Vishwamitra. As soon as Our eyes met, Our worlds changed.

“Remind Her of the words that resonate in My ears even now. When We were leaving the city of Ayodhya to dwell in the forest for fourteen years, Sita kept asking Me one question repeatedly. That question of Hers puzzled Me and at the same time delighted Me. She kept asking, ‘Have We reached the forest?’ Even as We were stepping out of the palace of Ayodhya, She asked Me if We had reached the forest. When We were at the city gates, She asked Me the same question again. When We reached the shores of Tamasa river, which marks the borders of the Ayodhya city, She repeated the question. No third person knows about this innocent interaction between Us.”248

Then Rama went on to describe in detail the beauty of Mother Sita. Rama described Sita’s physical beauty. So much was the trust that He had developed in Hanuman by this time that He didn’t even need to think twice before describing His wife’s beauty to another male. When Hanuman heard Rama words and closely observed the heavy feeling that was wearing Him down, a doubt arose in his heart. These thoughts had been plaguing him since the huge monkey army assembled together in millions. Why were millions wasting their time looking for one woman? Why was Rama so obsessed with a woman? She may be His wife, but was so much trouble worth it to rescue a single person whose whereabouts or status no one had any clue about?249

He shook off that disturbing thought, but made a mental note that he would never ask this question to an already disturbed Rama or an angry Lakshmana or even the indebted Sugriva. This was not the right time, place or circumstance for any such doubt-clearing sessions. The only way he could find an answer was by meeting Sita. Only after he had taken a look at Her once would he be able to ascertain if this endeavor was worth it, and if Rama was actually right about spending so much energy on tracing one woman.

Hanuman rose to his feet, held the ring between the index finger and thumb of his right hand and touched it reverentially to his head. He then safely tucked it away in his waistcloth. Hanuman then bent low and held Lord Rama’s feet. Lord Rama in turn bent down and touched Hanuman’s shoulders to lift him up. Remaining in that bent, respectful position still holding his feet, Hanuman spoke, “My Lord, please allow me to remain in this position. As long as I cling to Your feet, there is hope for me. The moment You lift me, You will leave me. As long as I am at Your feet, Your hands will remain on my shoulders. This is the safest position for a living entity. On the one hand, I have the fortune of holding Your feet and on the other hand, Your able arms are holding Me. Hope and assurance are both available to me in this position.”

Sugriva Returns a Favor

The great teachers explain that Hanuman’s ability to carry out the herculean service entrusted to him came from the strength of the ring that Rama had given him and from chanting the holy name of Rama. In fact, had Rama chosen any other monkey for this task, the result would have been the same. So Hanuman’s success in executing the orders came from the empowerment of Lord Rama. However, Rama chose him because of three factors: his good attitude, his desire to serve and Sugriva’s recommendation.

When Rama met him for the first time, Hanuman recommended Sugriva to Rama, and now Sugriva repaid that debt by recommending Hanuman to Rama. This is the sign of ideal relationships between well-wishing friends.

“I am relying on you Hanuman, bring Me good news soon!” Hanuman leaped into the sky as Rama bade him farewell. Hanuman flew over the ranks and zoomed southward, guiding the others along the path to proceed. The monkey chiefs directed their armies and soon joined Hanuman as he ventured south toward the unforgettable adventure awaiting them.

for good reason

Long after the armies left, Rama asked Sugriva a question to clarify a doubt that had been bothering Him the whole day. He thought it was important for Him to know right away.

“How did you describe every nook and corner of the world with such accuracy? It is intriguing to me that you have this endless fountain of knowledge about the whole world. How did you manage to remember so many details?” Rama asked with a curious smile.

Sugriva answered it in one word. “Fear!”

That’s it! What does he mean by that? Rama was very baffled now and really wanted to know how Sugriva had so much information.

“When Vali kicked me out of the kingdom, I escaped from the borders of Kishkinda to stay away from the atrocities of my brother. But as you know, Vali was not satisfied by just kicking me out of the kingdom. He wanted to kick the life out of me as well. To save myself, I was on a mad running spree. He chased me to every corner of the world. There is no direction I hadn’t run toward. There is no place on the face of the earth where I hadn’t sought asylum from Vali’s assaults. After running all over the world, I found my solution at the base of my own home! By the will of providence, I came to know of Matanga rishi’s curse. From then on, I began to reside in that holy mountain in the company of saints such as Hanuman. But while I was running through the world in great fear, my mind registered every single place and topographical detail. That’s when I understood that the mind works much faster and sharper in the face of the greatest of fears! Today I realized that everything happens in one’s life for a reason. I had always cursed those traumatic days of running away from my brother. But now I understand that the chase was actually preparing me for this service.”
